\mathcal{L}^{n}\left(\Omega\setminus\bigcup_{j=1}^{\infty}\overline{\Omega_{r_{j},\sigma,p_{j}}}\right)=0.

We set

f(x)\coloneqq\begin{cases}{\rm Aff}(x)\quad&\text{$x\not\in\bigcup_{j=1}^{\infty}\overline{\Omega_{r_{j},\sigma,p_{j}}}$}\\
f_{r_{j},\sigma,p_{j}}(x)\quad&\text{if $x\in p_{j}+r_{j}\overline{\Omega_{r_{j},\sigma,p_{j}}}$ for some $j$}\end{cases}

We then have that
f:\Omega\to\mathbb{R}
is continuous, (
6.7
), since the Lipschitz constant
\|\nabla f\|
is uniformly bounded in any
\Omega_{r_{j},\sigma,p_{j}}
we find that
f:\Omega\to\mathbb{R}
is indeed Lipschitz, and the Lipschitz constant

\|\nabla f\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\precsim_{a,C}1.

This establishes (
6.2
).

In view of (
6.11
) we have

|f-({\rm Aff}(x))|\precsim\sigma+\sigma{\rm diam\,}(\Omega)\quad\text{in $\Omega$}

For
\sigma
suitably small this implies (
6.5
).

In view of (
6.8
) combined with (
6.12
) we find that

{\rm dist\,}(df,\{A_{1},A_{2}\})\leq O(\sigma)\quad\text{a.e. in $\Omega$}

so again taking
\sigma\ll 1
we conclude (
6.1
).

As for (
6.3
) it follows from (
6.9
), and (
6.4
) follows from (
6.10
) with
\sigma\ll 1
.
∎

Iterating
Lemma

6.1
we obtain

Lemma 6.2

(Wiggle lemma for laminates of finite order)
.

Let
\nu\in\mathcal{L}(\Omega)
a laminate of finite order in some open set
\mathcal{O}
, as defined in
Definition

5.4
, i.e.

\nu=\sum_{\mathfrak{i}=1}^{\aleph}\lambda_{\mathfrak{i}}\delta_{A_{\mathfrak{i}}}

such that
\lambda_{\mathfrak{i}}\in[0,1]
and
\sum_{\mathfrak{i}}\lambda_{\mathfrak{i}}=1
with
A_{\mathfrak{i}}\neq A_{\mathfrak{j}}
for
\mathfrak{i}\neq\mathfrak{j}
.

Also assume that we have any affine linear map on
\mathbb{R}^{n}

{\rm Aff}(x):\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\mathbb{R}^{M}\\
\mathbb{R}^{M}\otimes\bigwedge\nolimits^{n-2}\mathbb{R}^{n}\\
\end{array}\right)

such that

d({\rm Aff}(x))\equiv\overline{\nu}.

Then for any
\varepsilon,\delta>0
there exists a piecewise affine mapping

f:\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\mathbb{R}^{M}\\
\mathbb{R}^{M}\otimes\bigwedge\nolimits^{n-2}\mathbb{R}^{n}\\
\end{array}\right)

such that

•

{\rm dist\,}(df,\{A_{1},\ldots,A_{\aleph}\})<\delta\quad\text{a.e. in $\Omega$}

•

\|\nabla f\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\precsim_{|A_{1}|,\ldots,|A_{\aleph}|,|\nabla{\rm Aff}|}1

•

We have

|\{x\in\Omega:|df(x)-A_{\mathfrak{i}}|<\delta\}|=\lambda_{\mathfrak{i}}|\Omega|

for any
\mathfrak{i}\in\{1,\ldots,\aleph\}

•

\left|{\rm Aff}(x)-f\right|<\varepsilon\quad\text{in $\Omega$}

and

f={\rm Aff}(x)\quad\text{on $\partial\Omega$}

Proof.

This can be proven by induction. The case
\aleph=1
is trivial,
\aleph=2
is
Lemma

6.1
. Assume now that we have
\tilde{f}
for some laminate of finite order

\tilde{\nu}=\sum_{\mathfrak{i}=1}^{\aleph}\lambda_{\mathfrak{i}}\delta_{A_{\mathfrak{i}}},

and
\nu
is obtained from
\tilde{\nu}
by an elementary splitting in
\mathcal{O}
, say

A_{\aleph}=(1-s)A_{\aleph+1}+sA_{\aleph+2}

\nu=\sum_{\mathfrak{i}=1}^{\aleph-1}\lambda_{\mathfrak{i}}\delta_{A_{\mathfrak{i}}}+\lambda_{\aleph}(1-\lambda)\delta_{A_{\aleph}}+\lambda\lambda_{\aleph}(1-s)\delta_{A_{\aleph+1}}+\lambda\lambda_{\aleph}s\delta_{A_{\aleph+2}}

Since
\tilde{f}
is piecewise affine linear we can restrict our attention to (at most countably many) open subsets
\tilde{\Omega}_{i}
where
d\tilde{f}\equiv A_{\aleph}
. In these each of these sets we take an open subset
\tilde{\tilde{\Omega}}_{i}\subset\tilde{\Omega}_{i}
such that

|\tilde{\Omega}_{i}\setminus\overline{\tilde{\tilde{\Omega}}_{i}}|=(1-\lambda)|\tilde{\Omega}_{i}|

We do not change
\tilde{f}
in
\tilde{\Omega}_{i}\setminus\overline{\tilde{\tilde{\Omega}}_{i}}
. Inside
\tilde{\tilde{\Omega}}
(observe that
\tilde{f}
is affine linear in this set) we apply use
Lemma

6.1
to replace
\tilde{f}
by some
g_{i}
such that

|\{x\in\tilde{\tilde{\Omega}}_{i}:|dg_{i}(x)-A_{\aleph+1}|<\delta\}|=(1-s)|\tilde{\tilde{\Omega}}_{i}|

and

|\{x\in\tilde{\tilde{\Omega}}_{i}:|dg_{i}(x)-A_{\aleph+2}|<\delta\}|=s|\tilde{\tilde{\Omega}}_{i}|

We set

f\coloneqq\begin{cases}g_{i}\quad&\text{in $\tilde{\tilde{\Omega}}_{i}$}\\
\tilde{f}\quad&\text{otherwise}\\
\end{cases}

Since
g=\tilde{f}
on
\partial\tilde{\tilde{\Omega}}_{i}
the resulting
f
is still Lipschitz, and since
|\tilde{f}-{\rm Aff}|\ll 1
and
|g_{i}-\tilde{f}|\ll 1
we find that
|f-{\rm Aff}|\ll 1
.

The remaining properties are now easy to check.
∎

Combining
Lemma

6.2
with
Lemma

5.8
, we obtain the following (cf.
[
12
, Theorem 3.1]
,
[
14
, Proposition 1]
).

Lemma 6.3

(wiggle lemma for
T_{\aleph}
-configurations)
.

Assume that
\left({P},({C}_{\mathfrak{i}})_{\mathfrak{i}=1}^{\aleph},({\kappa}_{\mathfrak{i}})_{\mathfrak{i}=1}^{\aleph}\right)
is a
T_{\aleph}
-configuration in the sense of
Definition

3.12
.

Denote the endpoints by

Z_{\mathfrak{k}}\coloneqq\phi_{\mathfrak{k}}(P,(C_{\mathfrak{i}})_{\mathfrak{i}=1}^{\aleph},(\kappa_{\mathfrak{i}})_{\mathfrak{i}=1}^{\aleph})

and the base points by

P_{\mathfrak{k}}\coloneqq\pi_{\mathfrak{k}}(P,(C_{\mathfrak{i}})_{\mathfrak{i}=1}^{\aleph},(\kappa_{\mathfrak{i}})_{\mathfrak{i}=1}^{\aleph})

Let
\mu\in(0,1)
such that
\mu\kappa_{\mathfrak{i}}>1
for any
\mathfrak{i}\in\{1,\ldots,\aleph\}
and
0<\lambda<\mu
.

Set

\tilde{Z}_{\mathfrak{k}}\coloneqq(1-\mu)P_{\mathfrak{k}}+\mu Z_{\mathfrak{k}}

and

\overline{Z}_{\mathfrak{k}}\coloneqq(1-\lambda)P_{\mathfrak{k}}+\lambda Z_{\mathfrak{k}}

Fix any
\mathfrak{k}\in\{1,\ldots,\aleph\}
and take any affine function
{\rm Aff}:\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\mathbb{R}^{M}\\
\mathbb{R}^{M}\otimes\bigwedge\nolimits^{n-2}\mathbb{R}^{n}\\
\end{array}\right)
such that

d{\rm Aff}=\overline{Z}_{\mathfrak{k}}.

Then for any open set
\Omega
and any
\delta>0
there exist a piecewise affine
f:\Omega\to\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\mathbb{R}^{M}\\
\mathbb{R}^{M}\otimes\bigwedge\nolimits^{n-2}\mathbb{R}^{n}\\
\end{array}\right)
such that

(1)

{\rm dist\,}(df,\{Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{\aleph},P_{1},\ldots,P_{\aleph}\})<\delta
a.e. in
\Omega

(2)

\|\nabla f\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\precsim_{\left|Z_{1}\right|,\ldots,\left|Z_{\aleph}\right|,\left|\nabla{\rm Aff}\right|}1
.

(3)

Assuming that
\tilde{Z}_{\mathfrak{i}}\neq\tilde{Z}_{\mathfrak{j}}
for
\mathfrak{i}\neq\mathfrak{j}
for each
\varepsilon>0
we can choose
f
so that

\left|\left\{x\in\Omega:\ |df(x)-\tilde{Z}_{\mathfrak{k}}|<\varepsilon\right\}\right|\geq\frac{\lambda}{\mu}|\Omega|

and

\left|\left\{x\in\Omega:\ |df(x)-\tilde{Z}_{\mathfrak{i}}|<\varepsilon\right\}\right|>0\quad\forall\mathfrak{i}

(4)

and

\left|\left\{x\in\Omega:{\rm dist\,}(df(x),\{Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{\aleph}\})>\delta\right\}\right|<\varepsilon|\Omega|

(5)

\sup_{\Omega}|f-{\rm Aff}|<\delta

(6)

f={\rm Aff}
on
\partial\Omega

Proof.

Fix
\mathfrak{k}
, set
f_{0}\coloneqq{\rm Aff}
.

Take from
Lemma

5.8

\sigma_{i}\coloneqq(1-\frac{\lambda}{\mu})\left(\sum_{\mathfrak{l}=1}^{\aleph}\mu_{\mathfrak{l};i}\delta_{P_{\mathfrak{l}}}+\sum_{\mathfrak{l}=1}^{\aleph}\lambda_{\mathfrak{l};i}\delta_{\tilde{Z}_{\mathfrak{l}}}\right)+\frac{\lambda}{\mu}\delta_{\tilde{Z}_{\mathfrak{k}}}

which is a laminate of finite order. In view of
Lemma

6.2
we find an piecewise affine Lipschitz function
f
arbitrarily close to
{\rm Aff}
and

\left|\left\{x\in\Omega:\ |df(x)-\tilde{Z}_{\mathfrak{k}}|<\varepsilon\right\}\right|\geq\frac{\lambda}{\mu}

and

\left|\left\{x\in\Omega:\ |df(x)-\tilde{Z}_{\mathfrak{i}}|<\varepsilon\right\}\right|>0

However we do not necessarily have

{\rm dist\,}(df,\{\tilde{Z}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{Z}_{\aleph}\})\ll 1

but instead

{\rm dist\,}(df,\{\tilde{Z}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{Z}_{\aleph},P_{1},\ldots,P_{\aleph}\})\ll 1

Since
P_{\mathfrak{l}}\neq\tilde{Z}_{\mathfrak{k}}
we can however ensure that

|\{x:{\rm dist\,}(df(x),\{\tilde{Z}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{Z}_{\aleph}\})\not\ll 1\}|=(1-\frac{\lambda}{\mu})\sum_{\mathfrak{l}=1}^{\aleph}\mu_{\mathfrak{l}}|\Omega|

∎

7.
The
T_{\aleph}
-theorem

The following is the essence of
[
12
]
, see also
[
14
, Proposition 2]
whose proof we can essentially follow.

Theorem 7.1
.

Let
\aleph\in{\mathbb{N}}
and
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
open convex set. Assume that
F\in C^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{M\times n},\mathbb{R})
and
K_{F}
as in (
2.6
).

Assume that
\left(\overline{P},(\overline{C}_{\mathfrak{i}})_{\mathfrak{i}=1}^{\aleph},(\overline{\kappa}_{\mathfrak{i}})_{\mathfrak{i}=1}^{\aleph}\right)
is a non-degenerate
T_{\aleph}
-configuration in the sense of
Definition

3.12
with

\overline{Z}_{\mathfrak{k}}\coloneqq\phi_{\mathfrak{k}}(P,(C_{\mathfrak{i}})_{\mathfrak{i}=1}^{\aleph},(\kappa_{\mathfrak{i}})_{\mathfrak{i}=1}^{\aleph})\in K_{F}

Denote by
\mathcal{M}_{\aleph}
be the manifold of
T_{\aleph}
-tuples close-by from
Proposition

3.16
, and assume
\mathcal{M}_{\aleph}\cap\mathcal{K}_{F}
satisfies the condition (C) from
Definition

4.1
.

There exists
P_{0}\in\{\overline{Z}^{1},\ldots,\overline{Z}^{n}\}^{\mathscr{R}c}
such
1
1

1

Indeed, one can adapt the proof to show this is actually true for any
P_{0}\in\{\overline{Z}^{1},\ldots,\overline{Z}^{n}\}^{\mathscr{R}c}
, but we do not need this so we don’t bother
that the following holds

{\rm Aff}:\Omega\to\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\mathbb{R}^{M}\\
\mathbb{R}^{M}\otimes\bigwedge\nolimits^{n-2}\mathbb{R}^{n}\\
\end{array}\right)
is a given affine function with
d{\rm Aff}\equiv P_{0}
.

Then for any
\delta>0
there exists
f=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}f^{\prime}\\
f^{\prime\prime}\end{array}\right)\Omega\to\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\mathbb{R}^{M}\\
\mathbb{R}^{M}\otimes\bigwedge\nolimits^{n-2}\mathbb{R}^{n}\\
\end{array}\right)
with the following properties

(1)

df\in K_{F}\cap\bigcup_{\mathfrak{k}=1}^{\aleph}B_{\delta}(\overline{Z}_{\mathfrak{k}})
a.e. in
\Omega

(2)

In particular
u\coloneqq f^{\prime}:\Omega\to\mathbb{R}^{M}
is Lipschitz and solves (
2.1
) in distributional sense

(3)

f^{\prime}={\rm Aff}^{\prime}
on
\partial\Omega
and
\sup_{\Omega}|f^{\prime}-{\rm Aff}^{\prime}|<\delta

(4)

f^{\prime}
is nowhere
C^{1}
in
\Omega
. If the
T_{\aleph}
-configuration is moreover wild in the sense of
Definition

3.12
then actually
\partial_{\alpha}f^{\prime}
is nowhere continuous in
\Omega
for each
\alpha\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
.

Proof.

Recall, for
\overline{\delta}
suitably small, we have the map
\pi_{\mathfrak{k}}:\mathcal{M}_{\aleph}\cap\mathcal{K}_{F}\cap\left(B_{\overline{\delta}}(\overline{Z}_{1})\times\ldots\times B_{\delta}(\overline{Z}_{\aleph})\right)
from
Definition

4.1
.

Take a strictly increasing sequence
(\lambda_{i})_{i=1}^{\infty}
,
\lim_{i\to\infty}\lambda_{i}=1
.

\Phi_{i}^{\mathfrak{k}}\coloneqq(1-\lambda_{i})\pi_{\mathfrak{k}}+\lambda_{i}\phi_{\mathfrak{k}}:\mathcal{M}_{\aleph}\cap\mathcal{K}_{F}\to\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\mathbb{R}^{M}\otimes\bigwedge\nolimits^{1}\mathbb{R}^{n}\\
\mathbb{R}^{M}\otimes\bigwedge\nolimits^{n-1}\mathbb{R}^{n}\\
\end{array}\right).

Of course we would prefer to
\lambda_{i}=1
, but then
\phi_{i}^{k}
is not a locally open mapping, so we need to assume
\lambda_{i}<1
and let
\lambda_{i}\to 1
(we assume that
\lambda_{i}
so that
\Phi_{i}^{k}
is indeed this locally open mapping by condition (C)). We shall also assume that
\lambda_{1}\approx 1
, so that we can apply
Lemma

6.3
at will. Lastly, observe that we have

\lim_{k\to\infty}\sum_{i=k}^{\infty}\left|\lambda_{i+1}-\lambda_{i}\right|=1-\lambda_{k}\xrightarrow{k\to\infty}0.

Let
(\mathcal{O}_{i})_{i=0}^{\infty}
be an increasing sequence of relative open subsets of
\mathcal{M}\cap\mathcal{K}\cap\left(B_{\overline{\delta}}(\overline{Z}_{1})\times\ldots\times B_{\delta}(\overline{Z}_{\aleph})\right)
,

\mathcal{O}_{i+1}\supset\overline{\mathcal{O}_{i}}\supset\mathcal{O}_{i}.

Since
(\overline{Z}_{1},\ldots,\overline{Z}_{\aleph})
are pairwise different we may assume that for each
i

U_{i,\mathfrak{k}}\coloneqq\Phi_{i}^{\mathfrak{k}}(\mathcal{O}_{i})

are pairwise disjoint,
U_{i,\mathfrak{k}}\cap U_{i,\mathfrak{j}}=\emptyset
(take possibly
\lambda
even closer to
1
and
\overline{\delta}
small enough).

We set

U_{i}\coloneqq\bigcup_{\mathfrak{k}=1}^{\aleph}\Phi_{i}^{\mathfrak{k}}(\mathcal{O}_{i}).

By condition
(C)
each
U_{i}\subset\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\mathbb{R}^{M}\otimes\bigwedge\nolimits^{1}\mathbb{R}^{n}\\
\mathbb{R}^{M}\otimes\bigwedge\nolimits^{n-1}\mathbb{R}^{n}\\
\end{array}\right)
is open.

From now on we fix any

P_{0}\in U_{0}

We are now inductively going to define piecewise affine maps
f_{i}:\Omega\to\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\mathbb{R}^{M}\\
\mathbb{R}^{M}\otimes\bigwedge\nolimits^{n-2}\mathbb{R}^{n}\end{array}\right)
with the properties

(1)

f_{i}
is a piecewise affine Lipschitz map

(2)

f_{i}={\rm Aff}
on
\partial\Omega

(3)

df_{i}(x)\in U_{i}
for a.e.
x\in\Omega

(4)

\|f_{i}-f_{i-1}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq 2^{-i}

Assuming
f_{i}
is already constructed we set

\Omega_{i,\mathfrak{i}}\coloneqq\text{``}\{x\in\Omega:df_{i}\in U_{i,\mathfrak{i}}\}\text{''}

by which we mean (since
f_{i}
is piecewise affine) the union of open sets where
f_{i}
is affine and
df_{i}\in U_{i,\mathfrak{i}}
. Since for each
i
the
U_{i,\mathfrak{i}}
are disjoint we have from
Item

3
that

\left|\Omega\setminus\bigcup_{\mathfrak{i}=1}^{\aleph}\Omega_{i,\mathfrak{i}}\right|=0.

(5)

We have

|\{x\in\Omega_{i-1,\mathfrak{i}}:df_{i}\in U_{i,\mathfrak{i}}\}|\geq\frac{\lambda_{i-1}}{\lambda_{i}}\left|\Omega_{i-1,\mathfrak{i}}\right|

(6)

and

|\{x\in\Omega_{i-1,\mathfrak{k}}:df_{i}\in U_{i,\mathfrak{i}}\}|>0

(7)

\int_{\Omega}|df_{i+1}-df_{i}|\precsim\frac{\lambda_{i+1}-\lambda_{i}}{\lambda_{1}}|\Omega|

We set
f_{0}={\rm Aff}
to start the induction, clearly this satisfies all the conditions.

Now assume we have constructed
f_{i}
. We are going to discuss the construction of
f_{i+1}
.

We can restrict our attention to
\Omega_{i,\mathfrak{i}}
for some fixed
\mathfrak{i}\in\{1,\ldots,\aleph\}
. And within
\Omega_{i,\mathfrak{i}}
we restrict our attention to some
\tilde{\Omega}_{i,\mathfrak{i}}
where
f_{i}
is affine, that is

df_{i}\equiv Q\in U_{i,\mathfrak{i}}.

Simply by subdividing
\tilde{\Omega}_{i,\mathfrak{i}}
we can assume that

(7.1)

{\rm diam\,}\tilde{\Omega}_{i,\mathfrak{i}}<\frac{1}{i}

By definition of
U_{i,\mathfrak{i}}
, there exists some
T\in\mathcal{O}_{i}
such that

Q=(1-\lambda_{i})\pi_{\mathfrak{i}}(T)+\lambda_{i}\phi_{\mathfrak{i}}(T)

For
\mathfrak{k}\in\{1,\ldots,\aleph\}
we set

Z^{\mathfrak{k}}\coloneqq(1-\lambda_{{i+1}})\pi_{\mathfrak{k}}(T)+\lambda_{{i+1}}\phi_{\mathfrak{k}}(T)\in U^{i+1}.

By
Lemma

5.8

Q\in\{Z^{1},\ldots,Z^{\aleph}\}^{\mathscr{R}c}

and we have an explicit laminate of finite order
\sigma_{\ell}

\sigma_{\ell}\coloneqq(1-\frac{\lambda_{i}}{\lambda_{i+1}})\left(\sum_{\mathfrak{l}=1}^{\aleph}\tilde{\mu}_{\mathfrak{l};\ell}\delta_{P_{\mathfrak{l}}}+\sum_{\mathfrak{l}=1}^{\aleph}\tilde{\lambda}_{\mathfrak{l};i}\delta_{Z_{\mathfrak{l}}}\right)+\frac{\lambda_{i}}{\lambda_{i+1}}\delta_{Z_{\mathfrak{i}}}

with
\overline{\sigma_{i}}=Q
and for
\ell\to\infty
the measures
\sigma_{\ell}
weak*-converges to a laminate

(1-\frac{\lambda_{i}}{\lambda_{i+1}})\sum_{\mathfrak{l}=1}^{\aleph}\tilde{\mu}_{\mathfrak{l}}\delta_{Z_{\mathfrak{l}}}+\frac{\lambda_{i}}{\lambda_{i+1}}\delta_{Z_{\mathfrak{i}}}.

Here

P_{\mathfrak{l}}=\pi_{\mathfrak{k}}(T).

It is important to observe that since
T\in\mathcal{O}_{i}
these are the endpoints of a
T_{\aleph}
-configuration which satisfies condition
(C)
, thus
\pi_{\mathfrak{k}}
is locally an open mapping, so there is a tiny
\delta>0
such that

(7.2)

B_{\delta}(P_{\mathfrak{l}})\subset\pi_{\mathfrak{k}}(\mathcal{O}_{i})\quad\forall\mathfrak{l}\in\{1,\ldots,\aleph\}.

We now apply
Lemma

6.3
and obtain a candidate for
df_{i+1}
in
\tilde{\Omega}_{i,\mathfrak{i}}
which satisfies properties (1), (2), (4), (5), (6).

We also have (for an
\varepsilon
we can choose)

(7.3)

\left|\left\{x\in\tilde{\Omega}_{i,\mathfrak{i}}:\ |df_{i+1}(x)-Z_{\mathfrak{i}}|<\varepsilon\right\}\right|\geq\frac{\lambda_{i}}{\lambda_{i+1}}|\tilde{\Omega}_{i,\mathfrak{i}}|

Observe that we have

|Z_{\mathfrak{i}}-Q|\precsim|\lambda_{i+1}-\lambda_{i}|

so that (choosing
\varepsilon
in (
7.3
) suitably small) we have

(7.4)

\int_{\tilde{\Omega}_{i,\mathfrak{i}}}|df_{i+1}-df_{i}|=\int_{\tilde{\Omega}_{i,\mathfrak{i}}}|df_{i+1}-Q|\precsim|\lambda_{i+1}-\lambda_{i}|\frac{\lambda_{i}}{\lambda_{i+1}}|\tilde{\Omega}_{i,\mathfrak{i}}|+(1-\frac{\lambda_{i}}{\lambda_{i+1}})|\tilde{\Omega}_{i,\mathfrak{i}}|\precsim\frac{\lambda_{i+1}-\lambda_{i}}{\lambda_{1}}|\tilde{\Omega}_{i,\mathfrak{i}}|

Below, we are not going to change
f_{i}
where
|df_{i}-Z_{\mathfrak{i}}|\ll 1
, so this statement will stay correct and (summing up over all
\tilde{\Omega}_{i,\mathfrak{i}}
) we have
Item

7
.

But, instead of
(3)
we have

{\rm dist\,}(df_{i+1},\{Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{\aleph},P_{1},\ldots,P_{\aleph}\})\ll 1\quad\text{a.e. in $\tilde{\Omega}_{i,\mathfrak{i}}$}.

However, we already know that the set where
df_{i+1}
is close to any of the
P_{\mathfrak{l}}
is very small. So we restrict again to subsets
\tilde{\tilde{\Omega}}_{i,\mathfrak{i}}
of
\tilde{\Omega}_{i,\mathfrak{i}}
where our new
f_{i+1}
is affine, and
df_{i+1}\asymp P_{\mathfrak{l}}
.
That is
df_{i+1}=\tilde{P}_{\mathfrak{l}}\in\pi_{\mathfrak{l}}(\mathcal{O}_{i})
, by (
7.2
). Thus there exists
\tilde{T}\in\mathcal{O}_{i}
such that

\tilde{P}_{\mathfrak{l}}=\pi_{\mathfrak{l}}(\tilde{T}).

Setting

\tilde{Z}^{\mathfrak{k}}\coloneqq(1-\lambda_{i+1})\pi_{\mathfrak{k}}(\tilde{T})+\lambda_{i+1}\phi_{\mathfrak{k}}(\tilde{T})\in U^{i+1}.

we have again from
Lemma

5.8
(this time with
\lambda=0
) that
P\in\{\tilde{Z}^{1},\ldots,\tilde{Z}^{\aleph}\}
with an explicit laminate, to which we can apply
Lemma

6.3
and replace
f_{i+1}
in
\tilde{\tilde{\Omega}}_{i,\mathfrak{i}}
. Observe this does not disturb any of the other (1)
\sim
(7). Iterating this argument we only replace
f_{i+1}
where
df_{i}
is not close to any
U^{i+1}
, and in the limit we find
df_{i+1}
satisfying also property yes).

Observe that the previous argument is essentially the same as what is usually used in
[
14
]
, namely that

(7.5)

U_{i}\subset U_{i+1}^{\mathscr{R}c}\quad\forall i\geq 0

We observe

\begin{split}&\int_{\Omega}|df_{i+1}-df_{i}|\\
=&\sum_{\mathfrak{i}=1}^{\aleph}\int_{\Omega_{i,\mathfrak{i}}}|df_{i+1}-df_{i}|\\
=&\sum_{\mathfrak{i}=1}^{\aleph}\int_{\Omega_{i,\mathfrak{i}},df_{i}\in U_{i+1,\mathfrak{i}}}|df_{i+1}-df_{i}|\\
&+\sum_{\mathfrak{i}=1}^{\aleph}\int_{\Omega_{i,\mathfrak{i}},df_{i}\not\in U_{i+1,\mathfrak{i}}}|df_{i+1}-df_{i}|\\
\end{split}

We now want to discuss the limiting procedure.

We observe that

\sup_{i}\|f_{i}-{\rm Aff}f_{i}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}<\infty

and

\sup_{i}\|df_{i}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}<\infty

But observe that we have no global Lipschitz bound!

If we write

f_{i}=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}f_{i}^{\prime}\\
f_{i}^{\prime\prime}\end{array}\right)\in\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\mathbb{R}^{M}\\
\mathbb{R}^{M}\otimes\bigwedge\nolimits^{n-2}\mathbb{R}^{n}\end{array}\right)

we observe that
f_{i}^{\prime}
is indeed Lipschitz,

\sup_{i}\|f_{i}^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}+[f_{i}^{\prime}]_{{\rm Lip\,}}<\infty

By
[
8
, Theorem 6.4]
there exist
g_{i}^{\prime\prime}
with
dg_{i}^{\prime\prime}=df_{i}^{\prime\prime}
such that for any
p<\infty

\sup_{i}\|g_{i}^{\prime\prime}\|_{W^{1,p}(\Omega)}<\infty

Set

g_{i}\coloneqq\left(\begin{array}[]{c}f_{i}^{\prime}\\
g_{i}^{\prime\prime}\end{array}\right),\quad\text{with }dg_{i}=df_{i}

Then (up to non-relabelled subsequence) there exist
g\in W^{1,p}(\Omega,\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\mathbb{R}^{M}\\
\mathbb{R}^{M}\otimes\bigwedge\nolimits^{n-2}\mathbb{R}^{n}\end{array}\right))
as a weak limit of
g_{i}
. By
Item

7
,
dg_{i}
is a Cauchy sequence in
L^{1}
and thus a.e. convergent. This implies

dg\in K_{F}\cap\bigcup_{\mathfrak{k}=1}^{\aleph}B_{\delta}(\overline{Z}_{\mathfrak{k}})\quad\text{a.e. in $\Omega$}

which implies that
g^{\prime}
solves (
2.1
) in distributional sense.
In particular, since
dg^{\prime}
is bounded, from (
2.5
)

dg^{\prime\prime i}=\star\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}(\partial_{X_{i\alpha}}F)(dg^{\prime})dx^{\alpha}

we see that
dg^{\prime\prime}
is bounded.

Moreover, if
g^{\prime}
was
C^{1}
then
dg^{\prime}
would be continuous, and thus, since we know (
2.5
)

dg^{\prime\prime i}=\star\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}(\partial_{X_{i\alpha}}F)(dg^{\prime})dx^{\alpha}.

we would conclude that
dg^{\prime\prime}
is continuous – that is we conclude that
dg
is continuous.

However,
dg
is not continuous
: Take any
\tilde{\Omega}\subset\Omega
, for some large
i_{0}
and some
\mathfrak{i}_{0}
some of the
\tilde{\Omega}_{i_{0},\mathfrak{i}_{0}}
from the construction of
f_{i}
must be a subset of
\tilde{\Omega}
, by (
7.1
).

By
Item

6
there is some
\gamma>0
(depending on
i_{0}
) such that

\left|\{x\in\tilde{\Omega}_{i_{0},\mathfrak{i}_{0}}:df_{i_{0}+1}(x)\in U_{i_{0}+1,\mathfrak{k}}\}\right|>\gamma\quad\forall\mathfrak{k}\in\{1,\ldots,\aleph\}.

But then by
Item

5
for any
i\geq i_{0}+1

\left|\{x\in\tilde{\Omega}_{i_{0},\mathfrak{i}_{0}}:df_{i}(x)\in U_{i,\mathfrak{k}}\}\right|\geq\frac{\lambda_{i-1}}{\lambda_{i}}\frac{\lambda_{i-2}}{\lambda_{i-1}}\ldots\frac{\lambda_{i_{0}}}{\lambda_{i_{0}+1}}\gamma=\frac{\lambda_{i_{0}}}{\lambda_{i}}\gamma.

Since
df_{i}
converges to
dg
strongly in
L^{1}
, we conclude that

\left|\{x\in\tilde{\Omega}_{i_{0},\mathfrak{i}_{0}}:dg(x)\in B_{\delta}(\overline{Z}_{\mathfrak{k}})\}\right|\geq\lambda_{i_{0}}\gamma.

This hold for any
\mathfrak{k}
, i.e. we have

\left|\{x\in\tilde{\Omega}:dg(x)\in B_{\delta}(\overline{Z}_{\mathfrak{k}})\}\right|>0\quad\forall\mathfrak{k}\in\{1,\ldots,\aleph\}.

and thus
dg
has non-vanishing essential oscillation, and in particular
dg
is not continuous.

If we write

\overline{Z}_{\mathfrak{k}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\overline{X}_{\mathfrak{k}}\\
\overline{Y}_{\mathfrak{k}}\end{array}\right)\in\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\mathbb{R}^{M}\otimes\bigwedge\nolimits^{1}\mathbb{R}^{n}\\
\mathbb{R}^{M}\otimes\bigwedge\nolimits^{n-1}\mathbb{R}^{n}\\
\end{array}\right)

and identify
X_{\mathfrak{k}}
with the canonical matrix in
\mathbb{R}^{M\times n}
, then we conclude that for any
\alpha\in(1,\ldots,n)
,

\left|\{x\in\tilde{\Omega}:\partial_{\alpha}g^{\prime}(x)\in B_{\delta}(\overline{X}_{\mathfrak{k}}e_{\alpha})\}\right|>0\quad\forall\mathfrak{k}\in\{1,\ldots,\aleph\}.

Assuming that
T_{\aleph}
is wild, and
\delta
is suitably small we conclude that
\partial_{\alpha}
has non-vanishing essential oscillation, and thus
\partial_{\alpha}
is not continuous in
\tilde{\Omega}
.
∎

8.
Polyconvex example: An extension method for
T_{\aleph}

The goal of this section is to find some
F:\mathbb{R}^{M\times n}\to\mathbb{R}
, which for algebra reasons we identify with

F:\mathbb{R}^{M}\otimes\bigwedge\nolimits^{1}\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}

such that there exists some
T_{\aleph}
-configuration,
(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{\aleph})\subset K_{F}
. For the application of the previous lemmata, in particular to vary
F
to obtain the condition (C),
Theorem

4.3
, we need
\aleph
to be large, and of course the
T_{\aleph}
-configuration must be degenerate in the space we are working in. The idea is to prove an extension theorem that extends the
M=n=2
-
T_{5}
-configuration obtained in
[
14
]
to arbitrary dimension and arbitrary
\aleph
(and ensuring non-degeneracy). This will be accomplished in
Proposition

8.5
.

Theorem 8.1
.

For any
M,n\geq 2
and
\aleph_{0}\in{\mathbb{N}}
there exists some
\aleph\geq\aleph_{0}
and a non-degenerate, wild
T_{\aleph}
-configuration in
\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\mathbb{R}^{M}\otimes\bigwedge\nolimits^{1}\mathbb{R}^{n}\\
\mathbb{R}^{M}\otimes\bigwedge\nolimits^{n-1}\mathbb{R}^{n}\\
\end{array}\right)

with endpoints

Z^{\mathfrak{i}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}X^{\mathfrak{i}}\\
Y^{\mathfrak{i}}\end{array}\right)

such

\{Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{\aleph}\}\subset K_{F}

Moreover, writing

Z_{\mathfrak{i}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}X_{\mathfrak{i}}\\
Y_{\mathfrak{i}}\end{array}\right)

we can ensure that
D^{2}F(X_{\mathfrak{i}})
is positive definite for each
\mathfrak{i}\in\{1,\ldots,\aleph\}
.

Recall (
2.6
)

K_{F}=\left\{\left(\begin{array}[]{c}X\\
Y\end{array}\right)\in\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\mathbb{R}^{M}\otimes\bigwedge\nolimits^{1}\mathbb{R}^{n}\\
\mathbb{R}^{M}\otimes\bigwedge\nolimits^{n-1}\mathbb{R}^{n}\\
\end{array}\right):\quad\left(\begin{array}[]{c}X\\
Y\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}X\\
\left(\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}\frac{\partial F}{\partial_{X_{i\alpha}}}(X){\star}dx^{\alpha}\right)_{i=1}^{M}\end{array}\right)\right\}

That is a configuration
Z=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}X\\
Y\end{array}\right)\in\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\mathbb{R}^{M}\otimes\bigwedge\nolimits^{1}\mathbb{R}^{n}\\
\mathbb{R}^{M}\otimes\bigwedge\nolimits^{n-1}\mathbb{R}^{n}\\
\end{array}\right)
belongs to
K_{F}
is equivalent to

(8.1)

Y^{{i}}=\frac{\partial F}{\partial_{X_{{i}{\alpha}}}}(X)\star dx^{{\alpha}}\in\bigwedge\nolimits^{n-1}\mathbb{R}^{n},\quad{i}=1,\ldots,M

For our purposes, we want to find
F
which is strongly polyconvex, cf.
Appendix

B
. That is, our
F
is supposed to have the form

(8.2)

F(X)=\frac{\varepsilon}{2}|X|^{2}+\delta G(\tilde{X}),

where

G:\mathbb{R}^{L}\to\mathbb{R}

for

L=\sum_{i=1}^{\min\{n,M\}}\binom{n}{i}\binom{M}{i}

which is the sum of the number of square
i\times i
-submatrices of an
\mathbb{R}^{M\times n}
matrix.
We will denote variables in
\mathbb{R}^{L}
by
\tilde{X}
. And (by a slight abuse of notation)
for

X=(X_{i\alpha})_{i=1,\ldots,M;\alpha=1,\ldots,n}\in\mathbb{R}^{M\times n}

and two multi-index
A=\{\alpha_{1}<i_{2}<i_{3}<\ldots<\alpha_{k}\}\subset\{1,\ldots,n\}
and
I=\{i_{1}<i_{2}<i_{3}<\ldots<i_{k}\}\subset\{1,\ldots,M\}
we set

X_{I,A}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times k}=\left(X_{i_{j},\alpha_{\beta}}\right)_{j=1,\beta}^{k},

and define

(8.3)

\tilde{X}\coloneqq\times_{k=1}^{\min\{M,n\}}\times_{|A|=|I|=k}\det_{k\times k}(X_{I,A})\in\mathbb{R}^{L}

In order to find
G
we recall the following simple existence result, cf.
[
14
]
.

Lemma 8.2
.

Assume we have

\left(\tilde{X}_{\mathfrak{i}}\right)_{\mathfrak{i}=1}^{\aleph}\subset\left(\mathbb{R}^{L}\right)^{\aleph}

with

(8.4)

\tilde{X}_{\mathfrak{i}}\neq\tilde{X}_{\mathfrak{j}}\quad\mathfrak{i}\neq\mathfrak{j},

and

(c_{\mathfrak{i}})_{\mathfrak{i}=1}^{\aleph}\subset\mathbb{R}^{\aleph}

and

(B_{\mathfrak{i}})_{\mathfrak{i}=1}^{\aleph}\subset(\mathbb{R}^{L})^{\aleph}

such that

(8.5)

c^{\mathfrak{j}}>c^{\mathfrak{i}}+\langle B_{\mathfrak{i}},\tilde{X}_{\mathfrak{j}}-\tilde{X}_{\mathfrak{i}}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{L}}\quad\forall\mathfrak{j}\neq\mathfrak{i}\in\{1,\ldots,{\aleph}\}

Then there exists a smooth convex function
G:\mathbb{R}^{L}\to\mathbb{R}
with

G(\tilde{X}_{\mathfrak{i}})=c^{\mathfrak{i}},\quad\mathfrak{i}=1,\ldots,\aleph

DG(\tilde{X}_{\mathfrak{i}})=B_{\mathfrak{i}},\quad\mathfrak{i}=1,\ldots,\aleph

Proof.

Set

G_{\mathfrak{i}}(\tilde{X})\coloneqq c^{\mathfrak{i}}+\langle B_{\mathfrak{i}},\tilde{X}-\tilde{X}_{\mathfrak{i}}\rangle\quad\tilde{X}\in\mathbb{R}^{L}

Then from the assumptions we have (
8.5
)

G_{\mathfrak{j}}(\tilde{X}_{\mathfrak{j}})=c_{j}>G_{\mathfrak{i}}(\tilde{X}_{\mathfrak{j}})\quad\forall\mathfrak{i}\neq\mathfrak{j}.

By continuity there exists
\delta>0
such that

G_{\mathfrak{j}}(\tilde{X})>G_{\mathfrak{i}}(\tilde{X})\quad\forall\mathfrak{i}\neq\mathfrak{j},\tilde{X}\in B(\tilde{X}_{\mathfrak{j}},\delta).

We set

G(\tilde{X})\coloneqq\max_{\mathfrak{i}=1,\ldots,\aleph}G_{\mathfrak{i}}(\tilde{X})

This is Lipschitz and convex and satisfies otherwise the assumptions, since

\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}Y_{12}&-Y_{11}\\
Y_{22}&-Y_{21}\\
\end{array}\right)G(\tilde{X})=G_{\mathfrak{j}}(\tilde{X})\quad\forall\tilde{X}\in B(\tilde{X}_{\mathfrak{j}},\delta)

By mollifying
G
outside of
\bigcup B(\tilde{X}_{\mathfrak{j}},\delta/2)
we conclude.
∎

The following Lemma reduces the job of finding
F
combined with a
T_{\aleph}
-tuple into a system of linear inequalities in
Y
. This was successfully employed in
M=n=2
-case by
[
14
]
.

Lemma 8.3
.

Assume that for some
\delta>0
we can find a tuple

\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\overline{X}^{\mathfrak{i}}\\
\hat{Y}^{\mathfrak{i}}\end{array}\right)\in\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\mathbb{R}^{M\times n}\\
\mathbb{R}^{M\times n}\\
\end{array}\right)\quad\mathfrak{i}\in\{1,\ldots,\aleph\},

and
(c^{\mathfrak{j}})_{\mathfrak{j}=1}^{\aleph}\subset\mathbb{R}
and
d_{k;A,I}^{\mathfrak{i}}\in\mathbb{R}
,
k\in\{1,\ldots,\min\{M,n\}\}
,
|A|=|I|=k
such that for all
\mathfrak{j}\neq\mathfrak{i}\in\{1,\ldots,{\aleph}\}
we have for any
\mathfrak{i}\neq\mathfrak{j}\in\{1,\ldots,\aleph\}
,

(8.6)

\begin{split}c^{\mathfrak{j}}>&c^{\mathfrak{i}}+\langle\hat{Y}^{\mathfrak{i}},\overline{X}^{\mathfrak{j}}-\overline{X}^{\mathfrak{i}}\rangle\\
&+\delta\sum_{k=2}^{\min\{M,n\}}\sum_{|A|=|I|=k}d^{\mathfrak{i}}_{k;A,I}\left(\det_{k\times k}(\overline{X}^{\mathfrak{j}}_{I,A})-\det(\overline{X}^{\mathfrak{i}}_{I,A})-\langle D_{X}\det_{I,A}(\overline{X}_{I,A}^{\mathfrak{i}}),\overline{X}^{\mathfrak{j}}_{I,A}-\overline{X}_{I,A}^{\mathfrak{i}}\rangle\right)\end{split}

Then for all suitably small
\varepsilon>0
there exists a convex map
G:\mathbb{R}^{L}\to\mathbb{R}
such that
F
as in (
8.2
)
we have

(8.7)

\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\overline{X}^{\mathfrak{i}}\\
\overline{Y}^{\mathfrak{i}}\end{array}\right)\in K_{F}\quad\mathfrak{i}=1,\ldots,\aleph.

where we identify
X^{\mathfrak{i}}_{i\alpha}\hat{=}X^{\mathfrak{i}}_{i\alpha}dx^{\alpha}
, and
where
\hat{Y}^{\mathfrak{i}}\in\mathbb{R}^{M\times n}
and
\overline{Y}^{\mathfrak{i}}\in\mathbb{R}^{M}\otimes\bigwedge\nolimits^{n-1}\mathbb{R}^{n}
are related by the identification

(8.8)

\overline{Y}^{\mathfrak{i}}=\left(\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}\hat{Y}^{\mathfrak{i}}_{i\alpha}\star dx^{\alpha}\right)_{i=1}^{M}.

If

d^{i}_{k;A;I}=0

whenever
A\neq(1,2)
or
I\neq(1,2)
, then
G=G(X,\det_{2\times 2}(X_{2x2}))
where
X_{2x2}
is the upper left square matrix of
X\in\mathbb{R}^{M\times n}
.

Remark 8.4
.

A word of warning: If we compare (
8.6
) to the corresponding system of inequalities for
M=n=2
from
[
14
]
, the matrix
\hat{Y}^{\mathfrak{i}}
above corresponds to
YJ
, where
J=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&-1\\
1&0\end{array}\right)
.

Proof of
Lemma

8.3
.

If we have (
8.6
), for suitably small
\varepsilon>0
, and changing the
c^{j}
we also have

(8.9)

\begin{split}\delta c^{\mathfrak{j}}>&\delta c^{\mathfrak{i}}+\langle\hat{Y}^{\mathfrak{i}},\overline{X}^{\mathfrak{j}}-\overline{X}^{\mathfrak{i}}\rangle\\
&-\varepsilon\langle\overline{X}^{\mathfrak{i}},\overline{X}^{\mathfrak{j}}-\overline{X}^{\mathfrak{i}}\rangle\\
&-\langle DF_{0}(\overline{X}^{\mathfrak{i}}),\overline{X}^{\mathfrak{j}}-\overline{X}^{\mathfrak{i}}\rangle\\
&+\delta\sum_{k=2}^{\min\{M,n\}}\sum_{|A|=|I|=k}d_{k;A,I}\left(\det_{k\times k}(\overline{X}^{\mathfrak{j}}_{I,A})-\det(\overline{X}^{\mathfrak{i}}_{I,A})-\langle D_{X}\det_{I,A}(\overline{X}_{I,A}),\overline{X}^{\mathfrak{j}}-\overline{X}^{\mathfrak{i}}\rangle\right)\end{split}

We define
d_{0}^{\mathfrak{i}}\in\mathbb{R}^{M\times n}
by

(8.10)

\delta d^{\mathfrak{i}}_{1}\coloneqq\hat{Y}^{\mathfrak{i}}-\varepsilon\overline{X}^{\mathfrak{i}}-DF_{0}(\overline{X}^{\mathfrak{i}})-\delta\sum_{k=2}^{\min\{M,n\}}\sum_{|A|=|I|=k}d_{k;A,I}^{\mathfrak{i}}\left(D_{X}\det_{I,A}\right)(\overline{X}_{I,A})

Then (
8.9
) becomes

(8.11)

\begin{split}\delta c^{\mathfrak{j}}>&\delta c^{\mathfrak{i}}+\delta\langle d_{1}^{\mathfrak{i}},\overline{X}^{\mathfrak{j}}-\overline{X}^{\mathfrak{i}}\rangle\\
&+\delta\sum_{k=2}^{\min\{M,n\}}\sum_{|A|=|I|=k}d_{k;A,I}^{\mathfrak{i}}\left(\det_{k\times k}(\overline{X}^{\mathfrak{j}}_{I,A})-\det(\overline{X}^{\mathfrak{i}}_{I,A})\right)\end{split}

For
\widetilde{\overline{X}^{\mathfrak{i}}}
as in (
8.3
), and

B^{\mathfrak{i}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}d_{1}^{\mathfrak{i}}\\
(d_{k;A,I}^{\mathfrak{i}})_{k=2,\ldots,\min\{M,n\},\ |A|=|I|=k}\end{array}\right)\in\mathbb{R}^{L}

the system (
8.11
) becomes

c^{\mathfrak{j}}>c^{\mathfrak{i}}+\langle B^{\mathfrak{i}},\widetilde{\overline{X}^{\mathfrak{j}}}-\widetilde{\overline{X}^{\mathfrak{i}}}\rangle

which is (
8.5
). Thus, by
Lemma

8.2
there exists
G:\mathbb{R}^{L}\to\mathbb{R}
convex with
DG(\widetilde{\overline{X}^{\mathfrak{i}}})=B^{\mathfrak{i}}
.

We now rewrite (
8.10
) and see that it is

\hat{Y}^{\mathfrak{i}}=\varepsilon\overline{X}^{\mathfrak{i}}+DF_{0}(\overline{X}^{\mathfrak{i}})+\delta D_{X}\Big|_{X=\overline{X}^{\mathfrak{i}}}\left(G\left(\widetilde{X}\right)\right),

that is

\hat{Y}^{\mathfrak{i}}_{i\alpha}=\frac{\partial}{\partial X_{i\alpha}}F(\overline{X}^{\mathfrak{i}}),\quad\mathfrak{i}=1,\ldots,\aleph;\ i=1,\ldots,M;\ \alpha=1,\ldots,n.

Thus

\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}\hat{Y}^{\mathfrak{i}}_{i\alpha}\star dx^{\alpha}=\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}\frac{\partial}{\partial X_{i\alpha}}F(\overline{X}^{\mathfrak{i}})\star dx^{\alpha},\quad\mathfrak{i}=1,\ldots,\aleph;\ i=1,\ldots,M.

Since we have by (
8.8
)

\overline{Y}^{i}=\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}\hat{Y}^{\mathfrak{i}}_{i\alpha}\star dx^{\alpha}\in\bigwedge\nolimits^{n-1}\mathbb{R}^{n}\quad\mathfrak{i}=1,\ldots,\aleph;\ i=1,\ldots,M.

taking into account (
8.1
), we have established (
8.7
).
∎

Theorem

8.1
is a consequence of
Proposition

8.5
below, combined with (
8.3
) and (
8.2
). The positive definiteness of
D^{2}F(X_{\mathfrak{i}})
can be obtained exactly as in
[
14
, p.145]
.

Proposition 8.5
.

For any
M,n\geq 2
and
\aleph_{0}\in{\mathbb{N}}
there exists some
\aleph\geq\aleph_{0}
and a non-degenerate and wild
T_{\aleph}
-configuration in
\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\mathbb{R}^{M}\otimes\bigwedge\nolimits^{1}\mathbb{R}^{n}\\
\mathbb{R}^{M}\otimes\bigwedge\nolimits^{n-1}\mathbb{R}^{n}\\
\end{array}\right)

with endpoints

Z^{\mathfrak{i}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}X^{\mathfrak{i}}\\
Y^{\mathfrak{i}}\end{array}\right)

such that we identify with matrices as in
Lemma

8.3
, then (
8.6
) holds for some choice of
c^{\mathfrak{i}}
,
d^{\mathfrak{i}}_{2,(1,2),(1,2)}\in\mathbb{R}
and all other
d^{\mathfrak{i}}_{k,A,I}=0
.

Let us remark, that the idea in
[
14
]
of solving (
8.6
) via a computer algebra system by “guessing good
X^{\mathfrak{i}}
” seems to work in any dimension and for any
\aleph
. We give an example for
M=n=3
in
Appendix

C
. Since we want to consider every dimension
M,n\geq 2
, and were unable to abstractly prove that (
8.6
) is solvable, we developed a method to extend the
M=n=2
-
T_{5}
-example to any dimension and any
\aleph
.

The main idea for
Proposition

8.5
is to extend the
T_{5}
in
\mathbb{R}^{4\times 2}
obtained in
[
14
]
to an
T_{\aleph}
with large
\aleph
and in any dimension
\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\mathbb{R}^{M\times n}\\
\mathbb{R}^{M\times n}\end{array}\right)
. The “lifting” into higher dimensions is relatively straight forward. The extension of a
T_{\aleph}
to a (useful)
T_{\aleph+2}
is more work.

In the following, since we work with both, the
\mathbb{R}^{4\times 2}
-situation and the
\mathbb{R}^{2M\times n}
-situation, we denote objects in the latter with
\underline{b}
etc.

First we discuss the lifting to higher dimensions.

Lemma 8.6
.

Let
M,n\geq 2
.
\aleph\geq 3
.

Assume we have a non-degenerate
T_{\aleph}
-configuration of
\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\mathbb{R}^{2\times 2}\\
\mathbb{R}^{2\times 2}\end{array}\right)

given by

(0,(C^{\prime\mathfrak{i}})_{\mathfrak{i}=1}^{\aleph},(\kappa_{\mathfrak{i}})_{\mathfrak{i}=1}^{\aleph})\in\mathbb{R}^{2\times 2}\times\left(\mathbb{R}^{2\times 2}_{{\rm rank\,}=1}\right)^{\aleph}\times\mathbb{R}^{\aleph}

(0,(C^{\prime\prime\mathfrak{i}})_{\mathfrak{i}=1}^{\aleph},(\kappa_{\mathfrak{i}})_{\mathfrak{i}=1}^{\aleph})\in\mathbb{R}^{2\times 2}\times\left(\mathbb{R}^{2\times 2}_{{\rm rank\,}=1}\right)^{\aleph}\times\mathbb{R}^{\aleph}

with endpoints

Z^{\mathfrak{i}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}X^{\mathfrak{i}}\\
Y^{\mathfrak{i}}\end{array}\right)=\sum_{\mathfrak{k}=1}^{\mathfrak{i}}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}C^{\prime\mathfrak{i}}\\
C^{\prime\prime\mathfrak{i}}\end{array}\right)+(\kappa_{\mathfrak{i}}-1)\left(\begin{array}[]{c}C^{\prime\mathfrak{i}}\\
C^{\prime\prime\mathfrak{i}}\end{array}\right),\mathfrak{i}=1,\ldots,\aleph,

where
C^{\prime\mathfrak{i}}=a^{\prime\mathfrak{i}}\otimes b^{\mathfrak{i}}
,
C^{\prime\prime\mathfrak{i}}=a^{\prime\prime\mathfrak{i}}\otimes b^{\mathfrak{i}}
for
a^{\mathfrak{i}}=(a^{\prime\mathfrak{i}},a^{\prime\prime\mathfrak{i}})^{T}\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
and
b^{\mathfrak{i}}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
,
|b^{\mathfrak{i}}|=1
.

Set, cf. (
3.3
),

\underline{C}^{\mathfrak{i}}=\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}\underline{b}^{\mathfrak{i}}_{;\alpha}dx^{\alpha}\wedge\underline{a}^{\mathfrak{i}}\in\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\mathbb{R}^{M}\otimes\bigwedge\nolimits^{1}\mathbb{R}^{n}\\
\mathbb{R}^{M}\otimes\bigwedge\nolimits^{n-1}\mathbb{R}^{n}\end{array}\right)

where

(8.12)

\underline{b}^{\mathfrak{i}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}b\\
0_{n-2}\end{array}\right)\in\mathbb{R}^{n},

(8.13)

\underline{a}^{\mathfrak{i}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\underline{a}^{\prime\mathfrak{i}}\\
\underline{a}^{\prime\prime\mathfrak{i}}\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}a^{\prime\mathfrak{i}}\\
0_{M-2}\end{array}\right)\\
\left(\begin{array}[]{c}a^{\prime\prime\mathfrak{i}}\\
0_{M-2}\end{array}\right)\star\left(dx^{1}\wedge dx^{2}\right)\end{array}\right)\in\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\mathbb{R}^{M}\\
\mathbb{R}^{M}\otimes\bigwedge\nolimits^{n-2}\mathbb{R}^{n}\end{array}\right).

Set

\underline{Z}^{\mathfrak{i}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\underline{X}^{\mathfrak{i}}\\
\underline{Y}^{\mathfrak{i}}\end{array}\right)=\sum_{\mathfrak{k}=1}^{\mathfrak{i}}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\underline{C}^{\prime\mathfrak{i}}\\
\underline{C}^{\prime\prime\mathfrak{i}}\end{array}\right)+(\kappa_{\mathfrak{i}}-1)\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\underline{C}^{\prime\mathfrak{i}}\\
\underline{C}^{\prime\prime\mathfrak{i}}\end{array}\right)\in\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\mathbb{R}^{M}\otimes\bigwedge\nolimits^{1}\mathbb{R}^{n}\\
\mathbb{R}^{M}\otimes\bigwedge\nolimits^{n-1}\mathbb{R}^{n}\end{array}\right)

Then

•

(0,(\underline{C}^{\mathfrak{i}})_{\mathfrak{i}=1}^{\aleph},(\kappa_{\mathfrak{i}})_{\mathfrak{i}=1}^{\aleph})
is a
T_{\aleph}
-configuration in
\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\mathbb{R}^{M}\otimes\bigwedge\nolimits^{1}\mathbb{R}^{n}\\
\mathbb{R}^{M}\otimes\bigwedge\nolimits^{n-1}\mathbb{R}^{n}\\
\end{array}\right)
the sense of
Definition

3.12

•

If
n=2
this
T_{\aleph}
-configuration is non-degenerate. If
n>2
the configuration satisfies all condition of nondegenerate
T_{\aleph}
condition except (
3.24
)

•

Assume that the
\mathbb{R}^{4\times 2}
-
T_{\aleph}
-configuration satisfies, for some
c^{\mathfrak{i}}\in\mathbb{R}
and
d^{\mathfrak{i}}\in\mathbb{R}
,

(8.14)

\begin{split}0>&c^{\mathfrak{i}}-c^{\mathfrak{j}}+\langle{Y}^{\mathfrak{i}}J,{X}^{\mathfrak{j}}-{X}^{\mathfrak{i}}\rangle+d^{\mathfrak{i}}\det(X^{\mathfrak{j}}-{X}^{\mathfrak{i}})\quad\text{for all $\mathfrak{i}\neq\mathfrak{j}\in\{1,\ldots,\aleph\}$}\end{split}

Here
J=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&-1\\
1&0\end{array}\right)
.

If we set

(8.15)

\underline{\hat{Y}}^{\mathfrak{i}}_{\cdot\alpha}=\star\left(dx^{\alpha}\wedge\underline{Y}^{\mathfrak{i}}\right)\in\mathbb{R}^{M},\quad\alpha\in\{1,\ldots,n\}

and identify

(8.16)

\mathbb{R}^{M\times n}\ni\underline{X}^{\mathfrak{i}}_{i\alpha}\hat{=}\underline{X}^{\mathfrak{i}}_{i\alpha}dx^{\alpha}\in\mathbb{R}^{M}\otimes\bigwedge\nolimits^{1}\mathbb{R}^{n}

then these matrices
(\underline{X}^{\mathfrak{i}},\underline{\hat{Y}}^{\mathfrak{i}})
satisfy (
8.6
) for
d^{\mathfrak{i}}_{2,(1,2),(1,2)}=d^{\mathfrak{i}}
and all other
d^{\mathfrak{i}}_{k,A,I}=0
.

Proof.

The first two bullet points are obvious, so is the last one. We just need to check (
8.6
).

Assume that
b=(b_{1},b_{2},0,\ldots,0)^{T}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
\underline{a}^{\prime\prime}=(a^{\prime\prime},0)^{T}\in\mathbb{R}^{M}
, for
a^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
and set

\underline{C}^{\prime\prime}\coloneqq\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}b_{\alpha}dx^{\alpha}\wedge\underline{a}^{\prime\prime}

We compute
\widehat{\underline{C}^{\prime\prime}}
as defined in (
8.15
):

\begin{split}&\star\left(dx^{\alpha}\wedge\left(\sum_{\beta=1}^{n}b_{\beta}dx^{\beta}\wedge\underline{a}^{\prime\prime}\right)\right)\\
=&\star\left(dx^{\alpha}\wedge\left(\sum_{\beta=1}^{n}b_{\beta}dx^{\beta}\wedge\left(\left(\begin{array}[]{c}a^{\prime\prime}\\
0_{M-2}\end{array}\right)\star\left(dx^{1}\wedge dx^{2}\right)\right)\right)\right)\\
=&\left(\begin{array}[]{c}a^{\prime\prime}\\
0_{M-2}\end{array}\right)\star\left(dx^{\alpha}\wedge\left(\sum_{\beta=1}^{2}b_{\beta}dx^{\beta}\wedge\left(\star\left(dx^{1}\wedge dx^{2}\right)\right)\right)\right)\\
\end{split}

This is zero for
\alpha\not\in\{1,2\}
. For
\alpha=1
,

\begin{split}&\star\left(dx^{1}\wedge\left(\sum_{\beta=1}^{n}b_{\beta}dx^{\beta}\wedge\underline{a}^{\prime\prime}\right)\right)\\
=&\left(\begin{array}[]{c}a^{\prime\prime}\\
0_{M-2}\end{array}\right)b_{2}\star\left(dx^{1}\wedge dx^{2}\wedge\left(\star\left(dx^{1}\wedge dx^{2}\right)\right)\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}a^{\prime\prime}\\
0_{M-2}\end{array}\right)b_{2}\end{split}

For
\alpha=2

\begin{split}&\star\left(dx^{2}\wedge\left(\sum_{\beta=1}^{n}b_{\beta}dx^{\beta}\wedge\underline{a}^{\prime\prime}\right)\right)\\
=&\left(\begin{array}[]{c}a^{\prime\prime}\\
0_{M-2}\end{array}\right)b_{1}\star\left(dx^{2}\wedge\left(dx^{1}\wedge\left(\star\left(dx^{1}\wedge dx^{2}\right)\right)\right)\right)\\
=&-\left(\begin{array}[]{c}a^{\prime\prime}\\
0_{M-2}\end{array}\right)b_{1}\end{split}

So we have

\widehat{\underline{C}^{\prime\prime}}=-\left(\begin{array}[]{c}a^{\prime\prime}\\
0_{M-2}\end{array}\right)\otimes\left(\begin{array}[]{c}b^{\perp}\\
0_{n-2}\end{array}\right)

Which by
Lemma

A.5
is equivalent to

\widehat{\underline{C}^{\prime\prime}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}\left(a^{\prime\prime}\otimes b\right)J&0&\ldots&0\\
0&0&\ldots&0\end{array}\right)

Thus,

\underline{\hat{Y}}^{\mathfrak{i}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}Y^{\mathfrak{i}}&0&\ldots&0\\
0&0&\ldots&0\end{array}\right)\in\mathbb{R}^{M\times n}

Thus it is clear that the claim is true.

∎

The basic idea to extend a given
T_{\aleph}
to a larger (and non-degenerate)
T_{\aleph+2}
is to distort two of the “rank-1” connections
C^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}},C^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\in\mathscr{R}^{o}
, and replace them by

C^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}+\delta\overline{C}

C^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}-\delta\overline{C}

Of course, in general neither of those two matrices above belongs to
\mathscr{R}^{o}
, but if
\overline{C}
is itself “rank 1” then each matrix above is “rank-2” i.e. each can be decomposed into two
\mathscr{R}^{o}
-connections. The point is that we can choose the two
\mathscr{R}^{o}
-connections in a way that the resulting
T_{\aleph+2}
-endpoints do not deviate too much from their original
T_{\aleph}
(this is the content of (
8.24
) below), and using the precise asymptotics as
\delta\to 0
we shall see that choosing
\overline{C}
wisely, we can ensure the linear system (
8.11
) remains valid.

We begin with the decomposition result. We state it first in
\mathbb{R}^{4\times 2}
. We are actually not going to use it, but just the
\mathbb{R}^{2M\times n}
-dimensional version, but we believe the
\mathbb{R}^{4\times 2}
-version is easier to process for first-time readers.

Lemma 8.7
.

Fix any matrix
C\in\mathbb{R}^{4\times 2}
,
\lambda\in(0,1)
,
a\in\mathbb{R}^{4}\setminus\{0\}
and
b\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
,
|b|=1
.

For any
\delta>0
exists two vectors
\tilde{b}^{\mathfrak{i}}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
,
|\tilde{b}^{\mathfrak{i}}|=1
, and
\tilde{a}^{\mathfrak{i}}\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
,
\mathfrak{i}=1,2
such that

•

We have

\delta C+a\otimes b=\tilde{a}^{1}\otimes\tilde{b}^{1}+\tilde{a}^{2}\otimes\tilde{b}^{2}

•

Any two vectors of
\{b,\tilde{b}^{1},\tilde{b}^{2}\}
are linearly independent.

•

\lim_{\delta\to 0}|\tilde{b}^{\mathfrak{i}}-b|=0
for
\mathfrak{i}=1,2
as
\delta\to 0

•

\lim_{\delta\to 0}|\tilde{a}^{1}-\lambda a|+|\tilde{a}^{2}-(1-\lambda)a|=0
.

•

If

C=\overline{a}\otimes\overline{b}\quad\overline{a}\in\mathbb{R}^{4},\quad\overline{b}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}

More precisely, as
\delta\to 0
, denoting by
b^{\perp}=(-b_{2},b_{1})^{T}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
, and
\sigma
either
\sqrt{\delta}
or
-\sqrt{\delta}
we can ensure that

\tilde{a}^{1}\otimes\tilde{b}^{1}-\lambda a\otimes b=\sigma\left(\lambda a\otimes b^{\perp}+(1-\lambda)|a|\langle b^{\perp},\overline{b}\rangle\overline{a}\otimes b\right)+o(\sqrt{\delta})

and

\tilde{a}^{2}\otimes\tilde{b}^{2}-(1-\lambda)a\otimes b=-\sigma\left(\lambda a\otimes b^{\perp}+(1-\lambda)|a|\langle b^{\perp},\overline{b}\rangle\overline{a}\otimes b\right)+o(\sqrt{\delta})

Here is the higher-dimensional version of
Lemma

8.7
. We leave it as an exercise to the reader to deduce
Lemma

8.7
from
Lemma

8.8
, or to prove
Lemma

8.7
by hand translating the arguments of
Lemma

8.8
.

Observe that we add
\delta^{2}\underline{\xi}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
which allows us to make our
T_{\aleph}
less flat, which is important to eventually obtain a non-degenerate
T_{\aleph}
, and ensure the
T_{\aleph}
is wild in the sense of
Definition

3.12
.

Lemma 8.8
.

Fix
\lambda\in(0,1)
,
\underline{C}\in\mathscr{R}^{o}
given by

\underline{C}=\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}\underline{b}_{\alpha}dx^{\alpha}\wedge\underline{a}\in\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\bigwedge\nolimits^{1}\mathbb{R}^{n}\\
\mathbb{R}^{M}\otimes\bigwedge\nolimits^{n-1}\mathbb{R}^{n}\end{array}\right),

for some
\underline{b}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
|\underline{b}|=1
and some
\underline{a}=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\underline{a}^{\prime}\\
\underline{a}^{\prime\prime}\end{array}\right)\in\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\mathbb{R}^{M}\\
\mathbb{R}^{M}\otimes\bigwedge\nolimits^{n-2}\mathbb{R}^{n}\end{array}\right)
.

Fix
\underline{\overline{a}}\in\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\mathbb{R}^{M}\\
\mathbb{R}^{M}\otimes\bigwedge\nolimits^{n-2}\mathbb{R}^{n}\end{array}\right)
and
\underline{\overline{b}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

We assume that

(8.17)

\underline{\overline{b}},\underline{b}\quad\text{linearly independent in $\mathbb{R}^{n}$}

in particular

(8.18)

p\coloneqq\underline{\overline{b}}-\langle\underline{\overline{b}},\underline{b}\rangle\underline{b}\neq 0.

Also let
\underline{\xi}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

For
\delta>0
set

\underline{\overline{C}}_{\delta}=\left(\underline{\overline{b}}_{\alpha}+\delta^{2}\underline{\xi}_{\alpha}\right)dx^{\alpha}\wedge\underline{\overline{a}}\in\mathscr{R}^{o}.

Then there exists two vectors
\underline{\tilde{b}}^{\mathfrak{i}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
|\underline{\tilde{b}}^{\mathfrak{i}}|=1
, and
\underline{\tilde{a}}^{\mathfrak{i}}\in\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\mathbb{R}^{M}\\
\mathbb{R}^{M}\otimes\bigwedge\nolimits^{n-2}\mathbb{R}^{n}\end{array}\right)
,
\mathfrak{i}=1,2
such that for

\underline{\tilde{C}}^{\mathfrak{i}}\coloneqq\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}\underline{\tilde{b}}^{\mathfrak{i}}_{\alpha}dx^{\alpha}\wedge\underline{\tilde{a}}^{\mathfrak{i}}\in\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\bigwedge\nolimits^{1}\mathbb{R}^{M}\\
\mathbb{R}^{M}\otimes\bigwedge\nolimits^{n-1}\mathbb{R}^{n}\end{array}\right)

•

we have the decomposition

(8.19)

\delta\underline{\overline{C}}_{\delta}+\underline{C}=\underline{\tilde{C}}^{1}+\underline{\tilde{C}}^{2}

•

For all small
\delta
, any two vectors of
\{\underline{b},\tilde{\underline{b}}^{1},\tilde{\underline{b}}^{2}\}
are linearly independent in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
,

•

For all small
\delta
, if we write
\underline{\tilde{a}}^{\mathfrak{i}}=\in\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\underline{\tilde{a}}^{\prime\mathfrak{i}}\\
\underline{\tilde{a}}^{\prime\prime\mathfrak{i}}\\
\end{array}\right)\in\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\mathbb{R}^{M}\\
\mathbb{R}^{M}\otimes\bigwedge\nolimits^{n-2}\mathbb{R}^{n}\end{array}\right)
then if
\{\underline{\overline{a}}^{\prime},\underline{\overline{a}}^{\prime\prime}\}
are linearly independent in
\mathbb{R}^{M}

then also

\{\underline{\tilde{a}}^{\prime 1},\underline{\tilde{a}}^{\prime 2}\}
are linearly independent in
\mathbb{R}^{M}
.

•

Recall
p
from (
8.18
) then

(8.20)

{\rm span\,}\{\underline{\tilde{b}}^{1},\underline{\tilde{b}}^{2}\}={\rm span\,}\{\underline{b}\}+{\rm span\,}\{p+\delta^{2}\left(\xi-\langle\xi,\underline{b}\rangle\underline{b}\right)\}

•

\lim_{\delta\to 0}|\tilde{\underline{b}}^{\mathfrak{i}}-\underline{b}|=0
for
\mathfrak{i}=1,2

•

\lim_{\delta\to 0}|\tilde{\underline{a}}^{1}-\lambda\underline{a}|+|\tilde{\underline{a}}^{2}-(1-\lambda)\underline{a}|=0
.

•

Indeed, we have the following asymptotics as
\delta\to 0

(8.21)

\begin{split}&\underline{\tilde{C}}^{1}-\lambda\underline{C}\\
=&\sqrt{\delta}\left(\frac{1}{2}\frac{1-\lambda}{1-\lambda+\lambda^{2}}\langle\underline{\overline{b}},\frac{p}{|p|^{2}}\rangle\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}\underline{b}_{\alpha}dx^{\alpha}\wedge\underline{\overline{a}}+\lambda\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}p_{\alpha}dx^{\alpha}\wedge\underline{a}\right)+o(\sqrt{\delta})\\
\end{split}

and

(8.22)

\begin{split}&\underline{\tilde{C}}^{2}-(1-\lambda)\underline{C}\\
=&-\sqrt{\delta}\left(\frac{1}{2}\frac{1-\lambda}{1-\lambda+\lambda^{2}}\langle\underline{\overline{b}},\frac{p}{|p|^{2}}\rangle\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}\underline{b}_{\alpha}dx^{\alpha}\wedge\underline{\overline{a}}+\lambda\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}p_{\alpha}dx^{\alpha}\wedge\underline{a}\right)+o(\sqrt{\delta})\\
\end{split}

Here recall (
8.18
) for the definition of
p
.

•

Fix
\kappa>1
and choose
\lambda\coloneqq\kappa-\sqrt{\kappa-\kappa}\in(0,1)
, i.e. such that

(8.23)

\tilde{\kappa}^{1}=\tilde{\kappa}^{2}\coloneqq\frac{\kappa}{\lambda}=\frac{\kappa-\lambda}{1-\lambda}>1

Then

(8.24)

\lim_{\delta\to 0}\left|\kappa\underline{C}-\tilde{\kappa}_{1}\underline{\tilde{C}}^{1}\right|+\left|\kappa\underline{C}-\left(\underline{\tilde{C}}^{1}+\tilde{\kappa}_{2}\underline{\tilde{C}}^{2}\right)\right|=0

Proof of
Lemma

8.8
.

Observe that
p\perp\underline{b}
and in view of (
8.17
)
0<|p|\leq 1
.

Define
\sigma=-\sqrt{\delta}\frac{\lambda}{1-\lambda}

\begin{split}\underline{\tilde{b}}^{1}\coloneqq&\frac{\underline{b}+\sqrt{\delta}\left(\underline{\overline{b}}+\delta^{2}\underline{\xi}-\langle\underline{\overline{b}}+\delta^{2}\underline{\xi},\underline{b}\rangle\underline{b}\right)}{\left|\underline{b}+\sqrt{\delta}\left(\underline{\overline{b}}+\delta^{2}\underline{\xi}-\langle\underline{\overline{b}}+\delta^{2}\underline{\xi},\underline{b}\rangle\underline{b}\right)\right|}\\
\overset{\eqref{eq:DQp}}{=}&\frac{\underline{b}+\sqrt{\delta}\left(p+\delta^{2}\underline{\xi}-\langle\delta^{2}\underline{\xi},\underline{b}\rangle\underline{b}\right)}{\left|\underline{b}+\sqrt{\delta}\left(p+\delta^{2}\underline{\xi}-\langle\delta^{2}\underline{\xi},\underline{b}\rangle\underline{b}\right)\right|}\\
=&\frac{\underline{b}+\sqrt{\delta}p}{\left|\underline{b}+\sqrt{\delta}p\right|}+O(\delta^{2})\\
=&\frac{\underline{b}+\sqrt{\delta}p}{\sqrt{1+\delta|p|^{2}}}\\
=&\underline{b}+\sqrt{\delta}p+\left(\underline{b}+\sqrt{\delta}p\right)\delta\left(\frac{|p|^{2}}{\sqrt{1+\delta|p|^{2}}\left(\sqrt{1+\delta|p|^{2}}+1\right)}\right)+O(\delta^{2})\\
=&\underline{b}+\sqrt{\delta}p+\delta\underline{b}|p|^{2}+\delta^{\frac{3}{2}}p|p|^{2}+O(\delta^{2})\\
\end{split}

Similarly,

\begin{split}\underline{\tilde{b}}^{2}\coloneqq&\frac{\underline{b}+\sigma\left(\underline{\overline{b}}+\delta^{2}\underline{\xi}-\langle\underline{\overline{b}}+\delta^{2}\underline{\xi},\underline{b}\rangle\underline{b}\right)}{\left|\underline{b}+\sigma\left(\underline{\overline{b}}+\delta^{2}\underline{\xi}-\langle\underline{\overline{b}}+\delta^{2}\underline{\xi},\underline{b}\rangle\underline{b}\right)\right|}\\
=&\frac{\underline{b}+\sigma\left(p+\delta^{2}\underline{\xi}-\langle\delta^{2}\underline{\xi},\underline{b}\rangle\underline{b}\right)}{\left|\underline{b}+\sigma\left(p+\delta^{2}\underline{\xi}-\langle\delta^{2}\underline{\xi},\underline{b}\rangle\underline{b}\right)\right|}\\
=&\underline{b}+\sigma p+\sigma^{2}\underline{b}|p|+\sigma^{3}p|p|^{2}+O(\delta^{2})\\
\end{split}

In particular,

•

Since
0\neq p\perp\underline{b}
we see that
(\underline{\tilde{b}}^{1},\underline{b})
and
(\underline{\tilde{b}}^{2},\underline{b})
are linearly independent for small
\sqrt{\delta}
. Since
\sigma\neq\sqrt{\delta}
we also see
(\underline{\tilde{b}}^{1},\underline{\tilde{b}}^{2})
must be linearly independent for small
\delta
.

•

Since
\sqrt{\delta}\neq\sigma
we have (
8.20
): Actually we have

(8.25)

\begin{split}{\rm span\,}\{\underline{\tilde{b}}^{1},\underline{\tilde{b}}^{2}\}=&{\rm span\,}\{\underline{b}\}+\hfil{\rm span\,}\{p+\delta^{2}\left(\xi-\langle\xi,\underline{b}\rangle\underline{b}\right)\}\\
\overset{\eqref{eq:DQp}}{=}&{\rm span\,}\{\underline{b}\}+\hfil{\rm span\,}\{\underline{\overline{b}}+\delta^{2}\xi\}\end{split}

Indeed,

{\rm span\,}\{\underline{\tilde{b}}^{1},\underline{\tilde{b}}^{2}\}\subset{\rm span\,}\{\underline{b}\}+{\rm span\,}\{p+\delta^{2}\left(\xi-\langle\xi,\underline{b}\rangle\underline{b}\right)\}

and

{\rm span\,}\{p+\delta^{2}\left(\xi-\langle\xi,\underline{b}\rangle\underline{b}\right)\}\subset{\rm span\,}\{\underline{\tilde{b}}^{1},\underline{\tilde{b}}^{2}\}+{\rm span\,}\{\underline{b}\}

are obvious from the definition of
\underline{\tilde{b}^{1}}
and
\underline{\tilde{b}^{2}}
.

On the other hand we can explicitly compute
\eta_{1},\eta_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
so that

\underline{b}=\eta_{1}\underline{\tilde{b}}^{1}+\eta_{2}\underline{\tilde{b}}^{2}.

We have

\underline{\tilde{b}}^{1}\frac{\sigma}{\sigma-\sqrt{\delta}}\left|\underline{b}+\sqrt{\delta}\left(\underline{\overline{b}}+\delta^{2}\underline{\xi}-\langle\underline{\overline{b}}+\delta^{2}\underline{\xi},\underline{b}\rangle\underline{b}\right)\right|=\frac{\sigma}{\sigma-\sqrt{\delta}}\underline{b}+\frac{\sigma\sqrt{\delta}}{\sigma-\sqrt{\delta}}\left(\underline{\overline{b}}+\delta^{2}\underline{\xi}-\langle\underline{\overline{b}}+\delta^{2}\underline{\xi},\underline{b}\rangle\underline{b}\right)\\

and

-\underline{\tilde{b}}^{2}\frac{\sqrt{\delta}}{\sigma-\sqrt{\delta}}\left|\underline{b}+\sigma\left(\underline{\overline{b}}+\delta^{2}\underline{\xi}-\langle\underline{\overline{b}}+\delta^{2}\underline{\xi},\underline{b}\rangle\underline{b}\right)\right|=\frac{-\sqrt{\delta}}{{\sigma-\sqrt{\delta}}}\underline{b}-\frac{\sqrt{\delta}\sigma}{\sigma-\sqrt{\delta}}\left(\underline{\overline{b}}+\delta^{2}\underline{\xi}-\langle\underline{\overline{b}}+\delta^{2}\underline{\xi},\underline{b}\rangle\underline{b}\right)

Adding the two we find (using
\underline{b}\perp p
)

\begin{split}\eta_{1}=&\frac{\sigma}{\sigma-\sqrt{\delta}}\left|\underline{b}+\sqrt{\delta}\left(\underline{\overline{b}}+\delta^{2}\underline{\xi}-\langle\underline{\overline{b}}+\delta^{2}\underline{\xi},\underline{b}\rangle\underline{b}\right)\right|\\
=&\frac{\sigma}{\sigma-\sqrt{\delta}}+0+O(\delta)\end{split}

and

\begin{split}\eta_{2}=&-\frac{\sqrt{\delta}}{\sigma-\sqrt{\delta}}+\left|\underline{b}+\sigma\left(\underline{\overline{b}}+\delta^{2}\underline{\xi}-\langle\underline{\overline{b}}+\delta^{2}\underline{\xi},\underline{b}\rangle\underline{b}\right)\right|\\
=&-\frac{\sqrt{\delta}}{\sigma-\sqrt{\delta}}+O(\delta).\end{split}

Since

\sigma=-\sqrt{\delta}\frac{\lambda}{1-\lambda},

we have the asymptotics

\begin{split}\eta_{1}=&\lambda+O(\delta)\end{split}

and

\begin{split}\eta_{2}=&(1-\lambda)+O(\delta).\end{split}

We already computed
\eta_{1},\eta_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
such that

\underline{b}=\eta_{1}\underline{\tilde{b}}^{1}+\eta_{2}\underline{\tilde{b}}^{2}.

By (
8.25
) there must be
\mu_{1},\mu_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
such that

(8.26)

\delta\left(\underline{\overline{b}}+\delta^{2}\underline{\xi}\right)=\mu_{1}\underline{\tilde{b}}^{1}+\mu_{2}\underline{\tilde{b}}^{2}\\

Thus

\begin{split}&\delta\overline{C}_{\delta}+\underline{C}\\
=&\delta\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}\left(\left(\underline{\overline{b}}_{\alpha}+\delta^{2}\underline{\xi}_{\alpha}\right)dx^{\alpha}\wedge\underline{\overline{a}}\right)+\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}\underline{b}_{\alpha}dx^{\alpha}\wedge\underline{a}\\
=&\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}\left(\mu_{1}\underline{\tilde{b}}^{1}+\mu_{2}\underline{\tilde{b}}^{2}\right)_{\alpha}dx^{\alpha}\wedge\underline{\overline{a}}+\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}\left(\eta_{1}\underline{\tilde{b}}^{1}+\eta_{2}\underline{\tilde{b}}^{2}\right)_{\alpha}dx^{\alpha}\wedge\underline{a}\\
=&\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}\underline{\tilde{b}}^{1}_{\alpha}dx^{\alpha}\wedge\mu_{1}\underline{\overline{a}}+\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}\underline{\tilde{b}}^{2}_{\alpha}dx^{\alpha}\wedge\mu_{2}\underline{\overline{a}}\\
&+\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}\underline{\tilde{b}}^{1}_{\alpha}dx^{\alpha}\wedge\eta_{1}\underline{a}+\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}\underline{\tilde{b}}^{2}_{\alpha}dx^{\alpha}\wedge\eta_{2}\underline{a}\\
=&\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}\underline{\tilde{b}}^{1}_{\alpha}dx^{\alpha}\wedge\left(\mu_{1}\underline{\overline{a}}+\eta_{1}\underline{a}\right)+\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}\underline{\tilde{b}}^{2}_{\alpha}dx^{\alpha}\wedge\left(\mu_{2}\underline{\overline{a}}+\eta_{2}\underline{a}\right)\\
\end{split}

So we set

\underline{\tilde{a}}^{1}\coloneqq\left(\mu_{1}\underline{\overline{a}}+\eta_{1}\underline{a}\right)

\underline{\tilde{a}}^{2}=\left(\mu_{2}\underline{\overline{a}}+\eta_{2}\underline{a}\right)

and we have found a decomposition as in (
8.19
).

We now need to discuss the asymptotics: We first observe

•

We have
\underline{\tilde{b}}^{1}-\underline{\tilde{b}}^{2}=(\sqrt{\delta}-\sigma)p+o(\sqrt{\delta})

•

Using
p\perp\underline{b}
we also find

\begin{split}\langle\underline{\tilde{b}}^{1},\underline{\tilde{b}}^{2}\rangle=&1+0+\left(\sqrt{\delta}\sigma+\delta+\sigma^{2}\right)|p|^{2}+0+O(\delta^{2})\\
=&1+\delta\left(\frac{\sqrt{\delta}\sigma+\delta+\sigma^{2}}{\delta}\right)\left|p\right|^{2}+0+O(\delta^{2})\\
\end{split}

and thus

\begin{split}\langle\underline{\tilde{b}}^{1},\underline{\tilde{b}}^{2}\rangle^{2}=&1+2\delta\left(\frac{\sqrt{\delta}\sigma+\delta+\sigma^{2}}{\delta}\right)\left|p\right|^{2}+O(\delta^{2})\\
\end{split}

It is worth noticing that for our choice of
\sigma
,
\frac{\sqrt{\delta}\sigma+\delta+\sigma^{2}}{\delta}\neq 0
.

We now compute
\mu_{1}
and
\mu_{2}
. Multiplying (
8.26
) with

\underline{\tilde{b}}^{1}-\langle\underline{\tilde{b}}^{1},\underline{\tilde{b}}^{2}\rangle\underline{\tilde{b}}^{2}\perp\underline{\tilde{b}}^{2}

we find

\begin{split}&\delta\langle{\underline{\overline{b}}+\delta^{2}\underline{\xi}},\underline{\tilde{b}}^{1}-\langle\underline{\tilde{b}}^{1},\underline{\tilde{b}}^{2}\rangle\underline{\tilde{b}}^{2}\rangle=\mu_{1}\left(1-\langle\underline{\tilde{b}}^{1},\underline{\tilde{b}}^{2}\rangle^{2}\right)\\
\Leftrightarrow&\langle{\underline{\overline{b}}+\delta^{2}\underline{\xi}},\underline{\tilde{b}}^{1}-\langle\underline{\tilde{b}}^{1},\underline{\tilde{b}}^{2}\rangle\underline{\tilde{b}}^{2}\rangle=\mu_{1}\left(2\left(\frac{\sqrt{\delta}\sigma+\delta+\sigma^{2}}{\delta}\right)\left|p\right|^{2}+O(\delta)\right)\\
\Leftrightarrow&\langle\underline{\overline{b}},\underline{\tilde{b}}^{1}-\underline{\tilde{b}}^{2}\rangle+O(\delta)=\mu_{1}\left(2\left(\frac{\sqrt{\delta}\sigma+\delta+\sigma^{2}}{\delta}\right)\left|p\right|^{2}+O(\delta)\right)\\
\Leftrightarrow&\langle\underline{\overline{b}},(\sqrt{\delta}-\sigma)p\rangle+o(\sqrt{\delta})=\mu_{1}\left(2\left(\frac{\sqrt{\delta}\sigma+\delta+\sigma^{2}}{\delta}\right)\left|p\right|^{2}+O(\delta)\right)\end{split}

Thus

\mu_{1}=\frac{1}{2}\frac{\sqrt{\delta}-\sigma}{\frac{\sqrt{\delta}\sigma+\delta+\sigma^{2}}{\delta}}\langle\underline{\overline{b}},\frac{p}{|p|^{2}}\rangle+o(\sqrt{\delta})

Observe that

\begin{split}&\frac{\sqrt{\delta}-\sigma}{\frac{\sqrt{\delta}\sigma+\delta+\sigma^{2}}{\delta}}\\
=&\sqrt{\delta}\frac{1+\frac{\lambda}{1-\lambda}}{\frac{\lambda}{1-\lambda}+1+\left(\frac{\lambda}{1-\lambda}\right)^{2}}\\
=&\sqrt{\delta}\frac{1-\lambda}{1-\lambda+\lambda^{2}}\\
\end{split}

Thus

\mu_{1}=\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\delta}\frac{1-\lambda}{1-\lambda+\lambda^{2}}\langle\underline{\overline{b}},\frac{p}{|p|^{2}}\rangle+o(\sqrt{\delta})

Multiplying (
8.26
) with

\underline{\tilde{b}}^{2}-\langle\underline{\tilde{b}}^{1},\underline{\tilde{b}}^{2}\rangle\underline{\tilde{b}}^{1}\perp\underline{\tilde{b}}^{1}

we get the same asymptotics only with a sign change for
\mu_{2}
,

\mu_{2}=-\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\delta}\frac{1-\lambda}{1-\lambda+\lambda^{2}}\langle\underline{\overline{b}},\frac{p}{|p|^{2}}\rangle+o(\sqrt{\delta})

These asymptotics in particular imply

•

\lim_{\delta\to 0}|\tilde{\underline{b}}^{\mathfrak{i}}-\underline{b}|=0
for
\mathfrak{i}=1,2

•

\lim_{\delta\to 0}|\tilde{\underline{a}}^{1}-\lambda\underline{a}|+|\tilde{\underline{a}}^{2}-(1-\lambda)\underline{a}|=0
.

•

We also observe that if
(\underline{\overline{a}}^{\prime},\underline{a}^{\prime})
are linearly independent vectors of
\mathbb{R}^{M}
then

\begin{split}&\zeta\underline{\tilde{a}}^{\prime 1}=\vartheta\underline{\tilde{a}}^{\prime 2}\\
\Leftrightarrow&\zeta\left(\mu_{1}\underline{\overline{a}}^{\prime}+\eta_{1}\underline{a}^{\prime}\right)=\vartheta\left(\mu_{2}\underline{\overline{a}}^{\prime}+\eta_{2}\underline{a}^{\prime}\right)\\
\Leftrightarrow&\mu_{1}\zeta\underline{\overline{a}}^{\prime}+\eta_{1}\zeta\underline{a}^{\prime}=\mu_{2}\vartheta\underline{\overline{a}}^{\prime}+\eta_{2}\vartheta\underline{a}^{\prime}\\
\Leftrightarrow&\left(\eta_{1}\zeta-\eta_{2}\vartheta\right)\underline{a}^{\prime}=\left(\mu_{2}\vartheta-\mu_{1}\zeta\right)\underline{\overline{a}}^{\prime}\\
\Leftrightarrow&\eta_{1}\zeta-\eta_{2}\vartheta=0\text{ and }\mu_{2}\vartheta-\mu_{1}\zeta=0\\
\Leftrightarrow&\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\eta_{1}&-\eta_{2}\\
-\mu_{1}&\mu_{2}\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\zeta\\
\vartheta\end{array}\right)=0\end{split}

We know that by

0\neq\langle\underline{\overline{b}},p\rangle\overset{\eqref{eq:DQp}}{=}\left|\underline{\overline{b}}\right|^{2}-\left(\langle\underline{\overline{b}},\underline{b}\rangle\right)^{2}\overset{\eqref{eq:barbbulbnotld}}{\neq}0,

we have the asymptotics

0\neq\mu_{2}=-\mu_{1}+o(\sqrt{\delta})

and

\eta_{1}=\lambda+o(\sqrt{\delta}),\quad\text{and}\quad\eta_{2}=(1-\lambda)+o(\sqrt{\delta}).

Since

\det\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\lambda&-(1-\lambda)\\
1&1\end{array}\right)=1\neq 0.

we find that for suitably small
\delta

\begin{split}&\zeta\underline{\tilde{a}}^{\prime 1}=\vartheta\underline{\tilde{a}}^{\prime 2}\\
\Leftrightarrow&\zeta=0\text{ and }\vartheta=0.\end{split}

Thus
\underline{\tilde{a}}^{\prime 1},\underline{\tilde{a}}^{\prime 2}
are linearly independent in
\mathbb{R}^{M}
.

Moreover,

\begin{split}&\underline{\tilde{C}}^{1}-\lambda\underline{C}\\
=&\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}\underline{\tilde{b}}^{1}_{\alpha}dx^{\alpha}\wedge\left(\mu_{1}\underline{\overline{a}}+\eta_{1}\underline{a}\right)-\lambda\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}\underline{b}_{\alpha}dx^{\alpha}\wedge\underline{a}\\
=&\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}\underline{\tilde{b}}^{1}_{\alpha}dx^{\alpha}\wedge\left(\mu_{1}\underline{\overline{a}}+\lambda\underline{a}\right)-\lambda\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}\underline{b}_{\alpha}dx^{\alpha}\wedge\underline{a}+O(\delta)\\
=&\mu_{1}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}\underline{\tilde{b}}^{1}_{\alpha}dx^{\alpha}\wedge\underline{\overline{a}}+\lambda\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}\left(\underline{\tilde{b}}^{1}-\underline{b}\right)_{\alpha}dx^{\alpha}\wedge\underline{a}+O(\delta)\\
=&\sqrt{\delta}\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{1-\lambda}{1-\lambda+\lambda^{2}}\langle\underline{\overline{b}},\frac{p}{|p|^{2}}\rangle\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}\underline{b}_{\alpha}dx^{\alpha}\wedge\underline{\overline{a}}+\lambda\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}p_{\alpha}dx^{\alpha}\wedge\underline{a}\right)+o(\sqrt{\delta})\\
\end{split}

and

\begin{split}&\underline{\tilde{C}}^{2}-(1-\lambda)\underline{C}\\
=&\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}\underline{\tilde{b}}^{2}_{\alpha}dx^{\alpha}\wedge\left(\mu_{2}\underline{\overline{a}}+\eta_{2}\underline{a}\right)-(1-\lambda)\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}\underline{b}_{\alpha}dx^{\alpha}\wedge\underline{a}\\
=&-\sqrt{\delta}\frac{1}{2}\frac{1-\lambda}{1-\lambda+\lambda^{2}}\langle\underline{\overline{b}},\frac{p}{|p|^{2}}\rangle\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}\underline{b}_{\alpha}dx^{\alpha}\wedge\underline{\overline{a}}+(1-\lambda)\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}\left(\sigma p\right)_{\alpha}dx^{\alpha}\wedge\underline{a}+o(\sqrt{\delta})\\
=&-\sqrt{\delta}\frac{1}{2}\frac{1-\lambda}{1-\lambda+\lambda^{2}}\langle\underline{\overline{b}},\frac{p}{|p|^{2}}\rangle\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}\underline{b}_{\alpha}dx^{\alpha}\wedge\underline{\overline{a}}-\lambda\sqrt{\delta}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}p_{\alpha}dx^{\alpha}\wedge\underline{a}+o(\sqrt{\delta})\\
=&-\sqrt{\delta}\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{1-\lambda}{1-\lambda+\lambda^{2}}\langle\underline{\overline{b}},\frac{p}{|p|^{2}}\rangle\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}\underline{b}_{\alpha}dx^{\alpha}\wedge\underline{\overline{a}}+\lambda\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}p_{\alpha}dx^{\alpha}\wedge\underline{a}\right)+o(\sqrt{\delta})\\
\end{split}

This establishes the asymptotic behavior in (
8.21
) and (
8.22
).

As for (
8.24
), by the previous results

\lim_{\delta\to 0}\left|\kappa\underline{C}-\tilde{\kappa}_{1}\underline{\tilde{C}}^{1}\right|=\lim_{\delta\to 0}\left|\kappa\underline{C}-\tilde{\kappa}_{1}\lambda\underline{C}\right|\overset{\eqref{eq:DQkappalpambda}}{=}0

Similarly

\lim_{\delta\to 0}\left|\kappa\underline{C}-\left(\underline{\tilde{C}}^{1}+\tilde{\kappa}_{2}\underline{\tilde{C}}^{2}\right)\right|=\lim_{\delta\to 0}\left|\kappa\underline{C}-\left(\lambda\underline{C}^{1}+\tilde{\kappa}_{2}(1-\lambda)\underline{C}^{2}\right)\right|\overset{\eqref{eq:DQkappalpambda}}{=}0

\frac{\kappa}{\lambda}=\frac{\kappa-\lambda}{1-\lambda}

\lim_{\delta\to 0}\left|\kappa\underline{C}-\tilde{\kappa}_{1}\underline{\tilde{C}}^{1}\right|+\left|\kappa\underline{C}-\left(\underline{\tilde{C}}^{1}+\tilde{\kappa}_{2}\underline{\tilde{C}}^{2}\right)\right|=0

We can conclude.
∎

The following is the main building block for
Proposition

8.5
.

Proposition 8.9
.

Assume for some given
\aleph\geq 3
there exists a
T_{\aleph}
-configuration
\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\mathbb{R}^{M}\otimes\bigwedge\nolimits^{1}\mathbb{R}^{n}\\
\mathbb{R}^{M}\otimes\bigwedge\nolimits^{n-1}\mathbb{R}^{n}\\
\end{array}\right)

which is non-degenerate except possibly for the spanning condition (
3.24
).
Let it be given by

(0,(C^{\prime\mathfrak{i}})_{\mathfrak{i}=1}^{\aleph},(\kappa_{\mathfrak{i}})_{\mathfrak{i}=1}^{\aleph})\in\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\mathbb{R}^{M}\otimes\bigwedge\nolimits^{1}\mathbb{R}^{n}\\
\end{array}\right)\times\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\mathbb{R}^{M}\otimes\bigwedge\nolimits^{1}\mathbb{R}^{n}\\
\end{array}\right)^{\aleph}\times(1,\infty)^{\aleph}

(0,(C^{\prime\mathfrak{i}})_{\mathfrak{i}=1}^{\aleph},(\kappa_{\mathfrak{i}})_{\mathfrak{i}=1}^{\aleph})\in\left(\mathbb{R}^{M}\otimes\bigwedge\nolimits^{n-1}\mathbb{R}^{n}\right)\times\left(\mathbb{R}^{M}\otimes\bigwedge\nolimits^{n-1}\mathbb{R}^{n}\right)^{\aleph}\times(1,\infty)^{\aleph}

with endpoints

Z^{\mathfrak{i}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}X^{\mathfrak{i}}\\
Y^{\mathfrak{i}}\end{array}\right)=\sum_{\mathfrak{k}=1}^{\mathfrak{i}}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}C^{\prime\mathfrak{i}}\\
C^{\prime\prime\mathfrak{i}}\end{array}\right)+(\kappa_{\mathfrak{i}}-1)\left(\begin{array}[]{c}C^{\prime\mathfrak{i}}\\
C^{\prime\prime\mathfrak{i}}\end{array}\right),\mathfrak{i}=1,\ldots,\aleph,

where

C^{\prime\mathfrak{i}}=b^{\mathfrak{i}}_{\alpha}dx^{\alpha}\wedge a^{\prime\mathfrak{i}}

C^{\prime\prime\mathfrak{i}}=b^{\mathfrak{i}}_{\alpha}\wedge a^{\prime\prime\mathfrak{i}}

for
a^{\mathfrak{i}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}a^{\prime\mathfrak{i}}\\
a^{\prime\prime\mathfrak{i}}\end{array}\right)\in\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\mathbb{R}^{M}\\
\mathbb{R}^{M}\otimes\bigwedge\nolimits^{n-2}\mathbb{R}^{n}\end{array}\right)
and
b^{\mathfrak{i}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
|b^{\mathfrak{i}}|=1
.

Assume it satisfies, with the identification (
8.15
) and (
8.16
), for some
c^{\mathfrak{i}}\in\mathbb{R}
and
d^{\mathfrak{i}}\in\mathbb{R}

(8.27)

\begin{split}c^{\mathfrak{j}}>&c^{\mathfrak{i}}+\langle\hat{Y}^{\mathfrak{i}},{X}^{\mathfrak{j}}-{X}^{\mathfrak{i}}\rangle+d^{\mathfrak{i}}\det_{2\times 2}({X}^{\mathfrak{j}}_{(1,2),(1,2)}-{X}^{\mathfrak{i}}_{(1,2),(1,2)})\end{split}

Assume there exists
\mathfrak{i}_{1}\neq\mathfrak{i}_{2}\in\{1,\ldots,\aleph\}
and a
\underline{\overline{a}}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{M}
,
\underline{\overline{a}}^{\prime\prime}=\underline{\overline{a}}_{\alpha\beta}\star\left(dx^{\alpha}\wedge dx^{\beta}\right)\in\mathbb{R}^{M}\otimes\bigwedge\nolimits^{n-2}\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
\underline{\overline{b}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
|\underline{\overline{b}}|=1
, such that if we set

\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}}\coloneqq\underline{\overline{b}}-\langle\underline{\overline{b}},\underline{b}^{\mathfrak{i}}\rangle\underline{b}^{\mathfrak{i}},

then we have

(8.28)

\langle\underline{\overline{b}},\frac{\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}}{|\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}|^{2}}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(\sum_{\alpha,\beta=1}^{n}\left(\langle\underline{a}^{\prime\prime\mathfrak{i}_{1}}_{\alpha\beta},\underline{\overline{a}}^{\prime}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{M}}-\langle\underline{\overline{a}}_{\alpha\beta}^{\prime\prime},\underline{a}^{\prime\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{M}}\right)\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}_{\beta}\underline{b}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}_{\alpha}\right)>-2d^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\langle\underline{\overline{b}},\frac{\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}}{|\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}|^{2}}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\ \langle\left(a^{\prime\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\right)^{\perp},\overline{a}^{\prime}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\,\langle\left(p^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\right)^{\perp},b^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}

and

(8.29)

\langle\underline{\overline{b}},\frac{\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}}{|\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}|^{2}}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(\sum_{\alpha,\beta=1}^{n}\left(\langle\underline{a}^{\prime\prime\mathfrak{i}_{2}}_{\alpha\beta},\underline{\overline{a}}^{\prime}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{M}}-\langle\underline{\overline{a}}_{\alpha\beta}^{\prime\prime},\underline{a}^{\prime\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{M}}\right)\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}_{\beta}\underline{b}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}_{\alpha}\right)<-2d^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\langle\underline{\overline{b}},\frac{\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}}{|\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}|^{2}}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\ \langle\left(a^{\prime\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\right)^{\perp},\overline{a}^{\prime}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\,\langle\left(p^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\right)^{\perp},b^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}

Here, on the right-hand side for a vector
\underline{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{M}
or
\underline{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
we call the restriction to the first two entries
v
. Namely,

a^{\prime}=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\underline{a}^{\prime}_{1}\\
\underline{a}^{\prime}_{2}\\
\end{array}\right),\quad b=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\underline{b}_{1}\\
\underline{b}_{2}\end{array}\right),\quad p=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\underline{p}_{1}\\
\underline{p}_{2}\end{array}\right)

And, for an
\mathbb{R}^{2}
-vector we set
v^{\perp}=(-v_{2},v_{1})^{T}
.
(We also observe with some relief that it is irrelevant if
C^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}=a^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\otimes b^{\mathfrak{i}}
or
C^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}=(-a^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}})\otimes(-b^{\mathfrak{i}})
)

Then there exists a
T_{\aleph+2}
-configuration

(0,(\tilde{C}^{\mathfrak{i}})_{\mathfrak{i}=1}^{\aleph},(\tilde{\kappa}_{\mathfrak{i}})_{\mathfrak{i}=1}^{\aleph})

which is still non-degenerate except possibly for the spanning condition (
3.24
), and it still satisfies (
8.27
), (
8.28
) and (
8.29
).
Also, if the original
T_{\aleph}
-configuration satisfies
\dim{\rm span\,}\{b^{1},\ldots,b^{\aleph}\}=L<n
then we can ensure that the new
T_{\aleph}
-configuration satisfies
\dim{\rm span\,}\{\underline{\tilde{b}}^{1},\ldots,\underline{\tilde{b}}^{\aleph+2}\}\geq L+1
.

Lastly, regarding the wildness of the
T_{\aleph}
: If the original
T_{\aleph}
-configuration was wild for some
\{\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{k}\}\subset\{1,\ldots,n\}
then so is the new
T_{\aleph+2}
-configuration, and indeed for any
\beta\in\{1,\ldots,n\}\setminus\{\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{k}\}
we can ensure that the new
T_{\aleph+2}
-configuration is wild for
\{\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{k},\beta\}
.

Proof.

W.l.o.g.
\mathfrak{i}_{1}<\mathfrak{i}_{2}
.

If
\dim{\rm span\,}\{b^{1},\ldots,b^{\aleph}\}<n
take
\underline{\xi}\in{\rm span\,}\{b^{1},\ldots,b^{\aleph}\}^{\perp}\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
|\underline{\xi}|=1
.

The orthogonality assumption on
\underline{\xi}
ignores the wildness claim, but it is easy to check that a slight rotation of
\underline{\xi}
ensures that the construction below improves the wildness.

Fix
\underline{\overline{a}}\in\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\mathbb{R}^{M}\\
\mathbb{R}^{M}\otimes\bigwedge\nolimits^{n-2}\mathbb{R}^{n}\end{array}\right)
and
\underline{\overline{b}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
\mathfrak{i}_{1}\neq\mathfrak{i}_{2}\in\{1,\ldots,\aleph\}
as in (
8.28
), (
8.29
).
Obviously
\overline{b}\neq 0
and
\overline{a}\neq 0
, so without loss of generality we may assume
|\overline{a}|=1
and
|\overline{b}|=1
. Since the inequalities (
8.28
) and (
8.29
) are strict, we can also assume that
\overline{a}^{\prime},\overline{b}
are each linearly independent with each
a^{\prime\mathfrak{i}}
,
b^{\mathfrak{i}}
, respectively.

Set for small
\delta>0

\underline{\overline{C}}_{\delta}=\left(\underline{\overline{b}}_{\alpha}+\delta^{2}\underline{\xi}_{\alpha}\right)dx^{\alpha}\wedge\underline{\overline{a}}\in\mathscr{R}^{o}.

The idea is to “replace”
\underline{C}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}
with
\underline{C}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}+\delta\underline{\overline{C}}_{\delta}
and
C^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}
with
\underline{C}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}-\delta\underline{\overline{C}}_{\delta}
, for
\delta
sufficiently small, so that the new endpoints
\tilde{Z}^{\mathfrak{i}}
are close to the original endpoints. Cf.
Figure

1
.

Figure 1.
The construction for (
8.30
): We replace
C^{2}+\delta\overline{C}_{\delta}
with
\tilde{C}^{2}+\tilde{C}^{3}
which adds one element to the
T_{\aleph+1}
-configuration but with only slightly shifted endpoints. At some other point we do the same with
-\delta\overline{C}_{\delta}
to cancel the global effect.

We apply
Lemma

8.8
for
\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\coloneqq\kappa^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}-\sqrt{\kappa^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}-\kappa^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}}\in(0,1)
and obtain

(8.30)

C^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}+\delta\overline{C}_{\delta}=:\tilde{C}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}+\tilde{C}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1},

and
\tilde{\kappa}_{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1}
and
\tilde{\kappa}_{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1}
.

Denote

\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}}\coloneqq\underline{\overline{b}}-\langle\underline{\overline{b}},\underline{b}^{\mathfrak{i}}\rangle\underline{b}^{\mathfrak{i}},

then we have the asymptotic expansion

(8.31)

\begin{split}&\underline{\tilde{C}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}-\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\underline{C}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\\
=&\sqrt{\delta}\left(\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}_{\alpha}dx^{\alpha}\wedge\underline{a}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{1-\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}}{1-\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}+\left(\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\right)^{2}}\langle\underline{\overline{b}},\frac{\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}}{|\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}|^{2}}\rangle\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}\underline{b}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}_{\alpha}dx^{\alpha}\wedge\underline{\overline{a}}\right)+o(\sqrt{\delta})\\
\end{split}

(8.32)

\begin{split}&\underline{\tilde{C}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1}-(1-\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}})\underline{C}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\\
=&-\sqrt{\delta}\left(\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}_{\alpha}dx^{\alpha}\wedge\underline{a}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{1-\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}}{1-\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}+\left(\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\right)^{2}}\langle\underline{\overline{b}},\frac{\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}}{|\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}|^{2}}\rangle\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}\underline{b}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}_{\alpha}dx^{\alpha}\wedge\underline{\overline{a}}\right)+o(\sqrt{\delta})\\
\end{split}

Similarly, from
Lemma

8.8
for
\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\coloneqq\kappa^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}-\sqrt{\kappa^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}-\kappa^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}}\in(0,1)
we obtain
\tilde{\kappa}_{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+1},\tilde{\kappa}_{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+2}>1
and a decomposition

(8.33)

\underline{C}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}-\delta\underline{\overline{C}}_{\delta}=:\underline{\tilde{C}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+1}+\underline{\tilde{C}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+2}.

Observe that this is like the
\mathfrak{i}_{1}
-case, just taking
-\underline{\overline{a}}
because of the different sign of the
-\delta\underline{\overline{C}}_{\delta}
. Then we have the asymptotic expansion

\begin{split}&\underline{\tilde{C}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+1}-\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\underline{C}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\\
=&\sqrt{\delta}\left(\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}_{\alpha}dx^{\alpha}\wedge\underline{a}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}{-}\frac{1}{2}\frac{1-\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}}{1-\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}+\left(\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\right)^{2}}\langle\underline{\overline{b}},\frac{\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}}{|\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}|^{2}}\rangle\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}\underline{b}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}_{\alpha}dx^{\alpha}\wedge\underline{\overline{a}}\right)+o(\sqrt{\delta})\\
\end{split}

and

\begin{split}&\underline{\tilde{C}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+2}-(1-\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}})\underline{C}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\\
=&-\sqrt{\delta}\left(\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}_{\alpha}dx^{\alpha}\wedge\underline{a}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}{-}\frac{1}{2}\frac{1-\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}}{1-\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}+\left(\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\right)^{2}}\langle\underline{\overline{b}},\frac{\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}}{|\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}|^{2}}\rangle\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}\underline{b}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}_{\alpha}dx^{\alpha}\wedge\underline{\overline{a}}\right)+o(\sqrt{\delta})\\
\end{split}

We define the new
T_{\aleph+2}
-configuration.

\begin{split}\underline{\tilde{C}}^{\mathfrak{i}}=&\underline{C}^{\mathfrak{i}},\ \tilde{\kappa}^{\mathfrak{i}}=\kappa^{\mathfrak{i}}\\
\underline{\tilde{C}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}=&\text{above},\ \tilde{\kappa}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}=\text{above}\\
\underline{\tilde{C}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1}=&\text{above},\ \tilde{\kappa}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1}=\text{above}\\
\underline{\tilde{C}}^{\mathfrak{i}+1}=&C^{\mathfrak{i}},\ \tilde{\kappa}^{\mathfrak{i}+1}=\kappa^{\mathfrak{i}}\quad\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1\leq\mathfrak{i}\leq\mathfrak{i}_{2}\\
\underline{\tilde{C}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+1}=&\text{above},\ \tilde{\kappa}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+1}=\text{above}\\
\underline{\tilde{C}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+2}=&\text{above},\ \tilde{\kappa}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+2}=\text{above}\\
\underline{\tilde{C}}^{\mathfrak{i}+2}=&C^{\mathfrak{i}}\quad\tilde{\kappa}^{\mathfrak{i}+2}=\kappa^{\mathfrak{i}}\quad\mathfrak{i}\geq\mathfrak{i}_{2}+1\end{split}

By construction, globally the
\delta\underline{\overline{C}}_{\delta}
-distortion cancels and we have

\sum_{\mathfrak{i}=1}^{\aleph+2}\underline{\tilde{C}}^{\mathfrak{i}}=\sum_{\mathfrak{i}=1}^{\aleph}C^{\mathfrak{i}}=0.

•

Neighboring
(\underline{\tilde{a}}^{\prime\mathfrak{i}},\underline{\tilde{a}}^{\prime\mathfrak{i}+1})
are still linearly independent in
\mathbb{R}^{M}
: the only places where we replaced them they are very close to being collinear with
\underline{a}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}
,
\underline{a}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}
, respectively – which are linearly independent with their respective predecessor and successor. And the two neighboring vectors are linearly independent by
Lemma

8.8
.

•

Similarly, the neighboring
\underline{\tilde{b}}^{\mathfrak{i}}
are still pairwise linearly independent.

•

By (
8.20
)

{\rm span\,}\{\underline{\tilde{b}}_{1},\ldots,\underline{\tilde{b}}_{\aleph+2}\}={\rm span\,}\{\underline{b}_{1},\ldots,\underline{b}_{\aleph}\}+{\rm span\,}\{\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}+\delta^{2}\underline{\xi}\}+{\rm span\,}\{\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}+\delta^{2}\underline{\xi}\}

If
\dim{\rm span\,}\{\underline{b}_{1},\ldots,\underline{b}_{\aleph}\}<n
we chose
\xi\perp{\rm span\,}\dim\{\underline{b}_{1},\ldots,\underline{b}_{\aleph}\}
, for suitably small
\delta
