{\rm span\,}\{\underline{b}_{1},\ldots,\underline{b}_{\aleph}\}\cap{\rm span\,}\{\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}+\delta^{2}\underline{\xi}\}=\{0\}

and thus

\dim{\rm span\,}\{\underline{\tilde{b}}_{1},\ldots,\underline{\tilde{b}}_{\aleph+2}\}\geq\dim{\rm span\,}\{\underline{b}_{1},\ldots,\underline{b}_{\aleph}\}+1.

It remains to show that the new
T_{\aleph+2}
-configuration still satisfies (
8.14
), (
8.28
), (
8.29
).

By constructions, the endpoints of our new
T_{\aleph+2}
-configuration did not change too much:

(8.34)

\begin{split}\underline{\tilde{Z}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}-\underline{Z}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}=&\tilde{\kappa}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\underline{\tilde{C}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}-\kappa^{\mathfrak{i}}\underline{C}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\\
\underline{\tilde{Z}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1}-\underline{Z}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}=&\underline{\tilde{C}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}+\tilde{\kappa}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1}\underline{\tilde{C}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1}-\kappa^{\mathfrak{i}}\underline{C}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\\
\underline{\tilde{Z}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+1}-\underline{Z}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}=&\delta\underline{\overline{C}}_{\delta}+\tilde{\kappa}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+1}\underline{\tilde{C}}^{i_{2}+1}-\kappa^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\underline{C}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\\
\underline{\tilde{Z}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+2}-\underline{Z}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}=&\delta\underline{\overline{C}}_{\delta}+\underline{\tilde{C}}^{i_{2}+1}+\tilde{\kappa}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+1}\underline{\tilde{C}}^{i_{2}+2}-\kappa_{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\underline{C}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\end{split}

Thus by the construction, (
8.24
),

|\underline{\tilde{Z}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}-\underline{Z}^{\mathfrak{i}}|+|\underline{\tilde{Z}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1}-\underline{Z}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}|+|\underline{\tilde{Z}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1}-\underline{\tilde{Z}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}|\xrightarrow{\delta\to 0}0.

|\underline{\tilde{Z}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+1}-\underline{Z}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}|+|\underline{\tilde{Z}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+2}-\underline{Z}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}|+|\underline{\tilde{Z}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+2}-\underline{\tilde{Z}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+1}|\xrightarrow{\delta\to 0}0.

All other
\underline{\tilde{Z}}^{\mathfrak{i}}
differ by at most
\delta\underline{\overline{C}_{\delta}}
from
\underline{Z}^{\mathfrak{i}}
,
\underline{Z}^{\mathfrak{i}-1}
or
\underline{Z}^{\mathfrak{i}-2}
.

Set

\gamma_{0}\coloneqq-\max_{\mathfrak{i}\neq\mathfrak{j}\in\{1,\ldots,\aleph\}}c^{\mathfrak{i}}-c^{\mathfrak{j}}+\langle\hat{Y}^{\mathfrak{i}},{X}^{\mathfrak{j}}-{X}^{\mathfrak{i}}\rangle+d^{\mathfrak{i}}\det_{2\times 2}({X}^{\mathfrak{j}}_{(1,2),(1,2)}-{X}^{\mathfrak{i}}_{(1,2),(1,2)})

and observe that by (
8.27
) we have
\gamma_{0}>0
.

Fix some
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}\in(-\frac{\gamma_{0}}{2},\frac{\gamma_{0}}{2})
, then we can define our new variables

\begin{split}\underline{\tilde{Z}}^{\mathfrak{i}}=&\underline{Z}^{\mathfrak{i}},\ \tilde{c}^{\mathfrak{i}}\coloneqq c^{\mathfrak{i}},\ \tilde{d}^{\mathfrak{i}}\coloneqq d^{\mathfrak{i}}\quad\mathfrak{i}\leq\mathfrak{i}_{1}-1\\
|\underline{\tilde{Z}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}-\underline{Z}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}|\ll&1,\ \tilde{c}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\coloneqq c^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}},\ \tilde{d}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\coloneqq d^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\\
|\underline{\tilde{Z}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1}-\underline{Z}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}|\ll&1,\ \tilde{c}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1}\coloneqq c^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}+\gamma_{1},\ \tilde{d}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1}\coloneqq d^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\\
|\underline{\tilde{Z}}^{\mathfrak{i}+1}-\underline{Z}^{\mathfrak{i}}|\ll&1,\ \tilde{c}^{\mathfrak{i}+1}\coloneqq c^{\mathfrak{i}},\ \tilde{d}^{\mathfrak{i}+1}\coloneqq d^{\mathfrak{i}}\quad\mathfrak{i}_{1}<\mathfrak{i}\leq\mathfrak{i}_{2}-1\\
|\underline{\tilde{Z}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+1}-\underline{Z}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}|\ll&1,\ \tilde{c}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+1}\coloneqq c^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}},\ \tilde{d}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+1}\coloneqq d^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\\
|\underline{\tilde{Z}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+2}-\underline{Z}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}|\ll&1,\ \tilde{c}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+2}\coloneqq c^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}+\gamma_{2},\ \tilde{d}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+2}\coloneqq d^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\\
|\underline{\tilde{Z}}^{\mathfrak{i}+2}-\underline{Z}^{\mathfrak{i}}|\ll&1,\ \tilde{c}^{\mathfrak{i}+2}\coloneqq c^{\mathfrak{i}},\ \tilde{d}^{\mathfrak{i}+2}\coloneqq d^{\mathfrak{i}}\quad\mathfrak{i}\geq\mathfrak{i}_{2}+1\\
\end{split}

For
\delta
small enough (depending on
\gamma_{0}
and
\left|\underline{\overline{a}}\right|
,
\left|\underline{\overline{b}}\right|
,
\left|\underline{\overline{\xi}}\right|
), we then have

0>\tilde{c}^{\mathfrak{i}}-\tilde{c}^{\mathfrak{j}}+\langle\hat{\underline{\tilde{Y}}}^{\mathfrak{i}},\underline{\tilde{X}}^{\mathfrak{j}}-\underline{\tilde{X}}^{\mathfrak{i}}\rangle+\tilde{d}^{\mathfrak{i}}\det_{2\times 2}(\tilde{X}^{\mathfrak{j}}_{(1,2),(1,2)}-{X}^{\mathfrak{i}}_{(1,2),(1,2)})

for all
\mathfrak{i}\neq\mathfrak{j}\in\{1,\ldots,\aleph\}
except when
\{\mathfrak{i},\mathfrak{j}\}=\{\mathfrak{i}_{1},\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1\}
or
\{\mathfrak{i},\mathfrak{j}\}=\{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+1,\mathfrak{i}_{2}+2\}
. (We drop the underline for
\underline{X}
if we only consider
2\times 2
-matrices).

We also observe, that for
\delta
suitably small we still have (
8.28
) and (
8.29
), e.g. for
\mathfrak{i}_{1}
and
\mathfrak{i}_{2}+1
.

That is, in order to conclude it remains to show (observe that
\tilde{c}^{i_{1}+1}=\tilde{c}^{i_{1}}+\gamma
etc.). We now drop the subscript in
\det_{2\times 2}
but it is always applied to the top-left square submatrix.

0>-\gamma_{1}+\langle\widehat{\underline{\tilde{Y}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}},\underline{\tilde{X}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1}-\underline{\tilde{X}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\rangle+d^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\det_{2\times 2}(\tilde{X}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1}-\tilde{X}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}})

0>\gamma_{1}+\langle\widehat{\underline{\tilde{Y}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1}},\underline{\tilde{X}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}-\underline{\tilde{X}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1}\rangle+d^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\det_{2\times 2}({\tilde{X}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}-{\tilde{X}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1})

0>-\gamma_{2}+\langle\widehat{\underline{\tilde{Y}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+1}},\underline{\tilde{X}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+2}-\underline{\tilde{X}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+1}\rangle+d^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\det_{2\times 2}({\tilde{X}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+2}-{\tilde{X}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+1})

0>\gamma_{2}+\langle\widehat{\underline{\tilde{Y}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+2}},\underline{\tilde{X}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+1}-\underline{\tilde{X}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+2}\rangle+d^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\det_{2\times 2}({\tilde{X}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+1}-{\tilde{X}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+2})

Equivalently,

-\langle\widehat{\underline{\tilde{Y}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1}},\underline{\tilde{X}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}-\underline{\tilde{X}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1}\rangle-d^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\det_{2\times 2}(\tilde{X}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}-\tilde{X}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1})>\gamma_{1}>\langle\widehat{\underline{\tilde{Y}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}},\underline{\tilde{X}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1}-\underline{\tilde{X}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\rangle+d^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\det_{2\times 2}(\tilde{X}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1}-\tilde{X}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}})

-\langle\widehat{\underline{\tilde{Y}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+2}},\underline{\tilde{X}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+1}-\underline{\tilde{X}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+2}\rangle-d^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\det_{2\times 2}(\tilde{X}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+1}-\tilde{X}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+2})>\gamma_{2}>\langle\widehat{\underline{\tilde{Y}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+1}},\underline{\tilde{X}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+2}-\underline{\tilde{X}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+1}\rangle+d^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\det_{2\times 2}(\tilde{X}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+2}-\tilde{X}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+1})

For both inequalities, it suffices to show

-\langle\widehat{\underline{\tilde{Y}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1}},\underline{\tilde{X}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}-\underline{\tilde{X}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1}\rangle-d^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\det_{2\times 2}(\tilde{X}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}-\tilde{X}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1})>\langle\widehat{\underline{\tilde{Y}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}},\underline{\tilde{X}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1}-\underline{\tilde{X}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\rangle+d^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\det_{2\times 2}(\tilde{X}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1}-\tilde{X}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}})

-\langle\widehat{\underline{\tilde{Y}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+2}},\underline{\tilde{X}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+1}-\underline{\tilde{X}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+2}\rangle-d^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\det_{2\times 2}(\tilde{X}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+1}-\tilde{X}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+2})>\langle\widehat{\underline{\tilde{Y}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+1}},\underline{\tilde{X}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+2}-\underline{\tilde{X}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+1}\rangle+d^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\det_{2\times 2}(\tilde{X}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+2}-\tilde{X}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+1})

Indeed, if we had shown the above inequalities there must be some
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
such that

-\langle\widehat{\underline{\tilde{Y}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1}},\underline{\tilde{X}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}-\underline{\tilde{X}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1}\rangle-d^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\det_{2\times 2}(\tilde{X}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}-\tilde{X}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1})>\gamma_{1}>\langle\widehat{\underline{\tilde{Y}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}},\underline{\tilde{X}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1}-\underline{\tilde{X}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\rangle+d^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\det_{2\times 2}(\tilde{X}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1}-\tilde{X}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}})

-\langle\widehat{\underline{\tilde{Y}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+2}},\underline{\tilde{X}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+1}-\underline{\tilde{X}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+2}\rangle-d^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\det_{2\times 2}(\tilde{X}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+1}-\tilde{X}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+2})>\gamma_{2}>\langle\widehat{\underline{\tilde{Y}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+1}},\underline{\tilde{X}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+2}-\underline{\tilde{Y}}\underline{\tilde{X}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+1}\rangle+d^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\det_{2\times 2}(\tilde{X}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+2}-\tilde{X}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+1})

and since
|\underline{\tilde{Z}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1}-\underline{\tilde{Z}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}|\ll 1
and
|\underline{\tilde{Z}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+2}-\underline{\tilde{Z}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+1}|\ll 1
for suitably small
\delta
we may assume that
|\gamma_{1}|+|\gamma_{2}|<\frac{\gamma_{0}}{2}
.

That is, we need to show (the square-determinants are symmetric)

(8.35)

\langle\widehat{\underline{\tilde{Y}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}}-\widehat{\underline{\tilde{Y}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1}},\underline{\tilde{X}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}-\underline{\tilde{X}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1}\rangle>2d^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\det_{2\times 2}(\tilde{X}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1}-\tilde{X}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}})

and

(8.36)

\langle\widehat{\underline{\tilde{Y}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+1}}-\widehat{\underline{\tilde{Y}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+2}},\underline{\tilde{X}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+1}-\underline{\tilde{X}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+2}\rangle>2d^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\det_{2\times 2}(\tilde{X}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+2}-\tilde{X}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+1})

From (
8.34
)

\begin{split}\underline{\tilde{Z}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}-\underline{\tilde{Z}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1}=&\left(\tilde{\kappa}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}-1\right)\underline{\tilde{C}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}-\tilde{\kappa}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1}\underline{\tilde{C}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1}\\
\overset{\eqref{eq:DQkappalpambda}}{=}&\left(\tilde{\kappa}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}-1\right)\left(\underline{\tilde{C}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}-\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\underline{C}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\right)-\tilde{\kappa}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1}\left(\underline{\tilde{C}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1}-(1-\lambda^{i_{1}})\underline{C}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\right)\end{split}

In view of (
8.31
), (
8.32
)

\begin{split}&\underline{\tilde{Z}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}-\underline{\tilde{Z}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1}\\
=&\sqrt{\delta}\left(\tilde{\kappa}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}+\tilde{\kappa}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1}-1\right)\left(\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\sum_{\beta=1}^{n}\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}_{\beta}dx^{\beta}\wedge\underline{a}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{1-\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}}{1-\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}+\left(\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\right)^{2}}\langle\underline{\overline{b}},\frac{\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}}{|\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}|^{2}}\rangle\sum_{\beta=1}^{n}\underline{b}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}_{\beta}dx^{\beta}\wedge\underline{\overline{a}}\right)+o(\sqrt{\delta})\\
\end{split}

By the identifications (
8.15
)

\begin{split}&\left(\widehat{\underline{\tilde{Y}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}}-\widehat{\underline{\tilde{Y}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1}}\right)_{\beta}\\
=&\sqrt{\delta}\left(\tilde{\kappa}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}+\tilde{\kappa}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1}-1\right)\Big(\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\sum_{\beta=1}^{n}\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}_{\beta}\star\left(dx^{\alpha}\wedge dx^{\beta}\wedge\underline{a}^{\prime\prime\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\right)\\
&\quad+\frac{1}{2}\frac{1-\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}}{1-\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}+\left(\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\right)^{2}}\langle\underline{\overline{b}},\frac{\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}}{|\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}|^{2}}\rangle\sum_{\beta=1}^{n}\underline{b}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}_{\beta}\star\left(dx^{\alpha}\wedge dx^{\beta}\wedge\underline{\overline{a}}^{\prime\prime}\right)\Big)\\
&+o(\sqrt{\delta})\\
\end{split}

We can write

\underline{a}^{\prime\prime\mathfrak{i}_{1}}=\underbrace{\underline{a}^{\prime\prime\mathfrak{i}_{1}}_{\alpha\beta}}_{\in\mathbb{R}^{M}}\star\left(dx^{\alpha}\wedge dx^{\beta}\right)

Then

\begin{split}\mathbb{R}^{M}\ni&\left(\widehat{\underline{\tilde{Y}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}}-\widehat{\underline{\tilde{Y}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1}}\right)_{\alpha}\\
=&\sqrt{\delta}\left(\tilde{\kappa}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}+\tilde{\kappa}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1}-1\right)\big(\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\sum_{\beta=1}^{n}\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}_{\beta}\underbrace{\underline{a}^{\prime\prime\mathfrak{i}_{1}}_{\alpha\beta}}_{\in\mathbb{R}^{M}}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{1-\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}}{1-\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}+\left(\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\right)^{2}}\langle\underline{\overline{b}},\frac{\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}}{|\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}|^{2}}\rangle\sum_{\beta=1}^{n}\underline{b}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}_{\beta}\underbrace{\underline{\overline{a}}_{\alpha\beta}^{\prime\prime}}_{\in\mathbb{R}^{M}}\big)+o(\sqrt{\delta})\\
\end{split}

With (
8.16
),

(8.37)

\begin{split}\mathbb{R}^{M}\ni&\left(\underline{\tilde{X}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}-\underline{\tilde{X}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1}\right)_{\alpha}\\
=&\sqrt{\delta}\left(\tilde{\kappa}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}+\tilde{\kappa}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1}-1\right)\big(\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}_{\alpha}\underbrace{\underline{a}^{\prime\mathfrak{i}_{1}}}_{\in\mathbb{R}^{M}}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{1-\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}}{1-\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}+\left(\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\right)^{2}}\langle\underline{\overline{b}},\frac{\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}}{|\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}|^{2}}\rangle\underline{b}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}_{\alpha}\underbrace{\underline{\overline{a}}^{\prime}}_{\in\mathbb{R}^{M}}\big)+o(\sqrt{\delta})\\
\end{split}

Observe that
a^{\prime\prime}_{\alpha\beta}
are necessarily antisymmetric, thus we have cancellations:
\sum_{\alpha,\beta}\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}_{\beta}\underline{a}^{\prime\prime\mathfrak{i}_{1}}_{\alpha\beta}\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}_{\alpha}=0
, and
\sum_{\alpha,\beta}\underline{b}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}_{\beta}\underline{\overline{a}}_{\alpha\beta}^{\prime\prime}\underline{b}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}_{\alpha}=0

Thus

\begin{split}&\langle\widehat{\underline{\tilde{Y}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}}-\widehat{\underline{\tilde{Y}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1}},{\tilde{X}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}-\tilde{X}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1}}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{M\times n}}\\
=&\delta\left(\tilde{\kappa}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}+\tilde{\kappa}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1}-1\right)^{2}\cdot\\
\quad&\cdot\sum_{\alpha,\beta=1}^{n}\big\langle\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}_{\beta}\underline{a}^{\prime\prime\mathfrak{i}_{1}}_{\alpha\beta}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{1-\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}}{1-\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}+\left(\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\right)^{2}}\langle\underline{\overline{b}},\frac{\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}}{|\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}|^{2}}\rangle\underline{b}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}_{\beta}\underline{\overline{a}}_{\alpha\beta}^{\prime\prime},\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}_{\alpha}\underline{a}^{\prime\mathfrak{i}_{1}}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{1-\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}}{1-\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}+\left(\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\right)^{2}}\langle\underline{\overline{b}},\frac{\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}}{|\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}|^{2}}\rangle\underline{b}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}_{\alpha}\underline{\overline{a}}^{\prime}\Big\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{M}}+o(\delta)\\
=&\delta\left(\tilde{\kappa}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}+\tilde{\kappa}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1}-1\right)^{2}\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\frac{1}{2}\frac{1-\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}}{1-\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}+\left(\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\right)^{2}}\langle\underline{\overline{b}},\frac{\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}}{|\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}|^{2}}\rangle\left(\sum_{\alpha,\beta=1}^{n}\langle\underline{a}^{\prime\prime\mathfrak{i}_{1}}_{\alpha\beta},\underline{\overline{a}}^{\prime}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{M}}\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}_{\beta}\underline{b}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}_{\alpha}+\sum_{\alpha,\beta=1}^{n}\langle\underline{\overline{a}}_{\alpha\beta}^{\prime\prime},\underline{a}^{\prime\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{M}}\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}_{\alpha}\underline{b}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}_{\beta}\right)\\
&+o(\delta)\\
=&\delta\left(\tilde{\kappa}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}+\tilde{\kappa}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1}-1\right)^{2}\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\frac{1}{2}\frac{1-\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}}{1-\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}+\left(\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\right)^{2}}\langle\underline{\overline{b}},\frac{\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}}{|\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}|^{2}}\rangle\left(\sum_{\alpha,\beta=1}^{n}\left(\langle\underline{a}^{\prime\prime\mathfrak{i}_{1}}_{\alpha\beta},\underline{\overline{a}}^{\prime}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{M}}-\sum_{\alpha,\beta=1}^{n}\langle\underline{\overline{a}}_{\alpha\beta}^{\prime\prime},\underline{a}^{\prime\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{M}}\right)\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}_{\beta}\underline{b}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}_{\alpha}\right)\\
&+o(\delta).\end{split}

Thus

\begin{split}&\langle\widehat{\underline{\tilde{Y}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}}-\widehat{\underline{\tilde{Y}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1}},{\tilde{X}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}-\tilde{X}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1}}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{M\times n}}\\
=&\delta\left(\tilde{\kappa}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}+\tilde{\kappa}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1}-1\right)^{2}\cdot\\
\quad&\cdot\sum_{\alpha,\beta=1}^{n}\big\langle\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}_{\beta}\underline{a}^{\prime\prime\mathfrak{i}_{1}}_{\alpha\beta}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{1-\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}}{1-\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}+\left(\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\right)^{2}}\langle\underline{\overline{b}},\frac{\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}}{|\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}|^{2}}\rangle\underline{b}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}_{\beta}\underline{\overline{a}}_{\alpha\beta}^{\prime\prime},\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}_{\alpha}\underline{a}^{\prime\mathfrak{i}_{1}}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{1-\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}}{1-\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}+\left(\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\right)^{2}}\langle\underline{\overline{b}},\frac{\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}}{|\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}|^{2}}\rangle\underline{b}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}_{\alpha}\underline{\overline{a}}^{\prime}\Big\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{M}}+o(\delta)\\
=&\delta\left(\tilde{\kappa}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}+\tilde{\kappa}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1}-1\right)^{2}\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\frac{1}{2}\frac{1-\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}}{1-\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}+\left(\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\right)^{2}}\langle\underline{\overline{b}},\frac{\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}}{|\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}|^{2}}\rangle\left(\sum_{\alpha,\beta=1}^{n}\langle\underline{a}^{\prime\prime\mathfrak{i}_{1}}_{\alpha\beta},\underline{\overline{a}}^{\prime}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{M}}\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}_{\beta}\underline{b}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}_{\alpha}+\sum_{\alpha,\beta=1}^{n}\langle\underline{\overline{a}}_{\alpha\beta}^{\prime\prime},\underline{a}^{\prime\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{M}}\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}_{\alpha}\underline{b}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}_{\beta}\right)\\
&+o(\delta)\\
=&\delta\left(\tilde{\kappa}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}+\tilde{\kappa}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1}-1\right)^{2}\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\frac{1}{2}\frac{1-\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}}{1-\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}+\left(\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\right)^{2}}\langle\underline{\overline{b}},\frac{\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}}{|\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}|^{2}}\rangle\left(\sum_{\alpha,\beta=1}^{n}\left(\langle\underline{a}^{\prime\prime\mathfrak{i}_{1}}_{\alpha\beta},\underline{\overline{a}}^{\prime}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{M}}-\langle\underline{\overline{a}}_{\alpha\beta}^{\prime\prime},\underline{a}^{\prime\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{M}}\right)\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}_{\beta}\underline{b}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}_{\alpha}\right)\\
&+o(\delta).\end{split}

Similarly (recall that this case is just taking
-\underline{\overline{a}}
instead of
\underline{\overline{a}}
)

\begin{split}&\langle\widehat{\underline{\tilde{Y}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+1}}-\widehat{\underline{\tilde{Y}}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+2}},{\tilde{X}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+1}-\tilde{X}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+2}}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{M\times n}}\\
=&{-}\delta\left(\tilde{\kappa}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+1}+\tilde{\kappa}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+2}-1\right)^{2}\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\frac{1}{2}\frac{1-\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}}{1-\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\left(\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\right)^{2}}\langle\underline{\overline{b}},\frac{\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}}{|\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}|^{2}}\rangle\left(\sum_{\alpha,\beta=1}^{n}\left(\langle\underline{a}^{\prime\prime\mathfrak{i}_{2}}_{\alpha\beta},\underline{\overline{a}}^{\prime}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{M}}-\langle\underline{\overline{a}}_{\alpha\beta}^{\prime\prime},\underline{a}^{\prime\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{M}}\right)\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}_{\beta}\underline{b}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}_{\alpha}\right)\\
&+o(\delta).\end{split}

Next, we go for the
2\times 2
determinant term:
For a vector
\underline{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{M}
or
\underline{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
we call the restriction to the first two entries
v
. Namely,

a^{\prime}=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\underline{a}^{\prime}_{1}\\
\underline{a}^{\prime}_{2}\\
\end{array}\right),\quad b=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\underline{b}_{1}\\
\underline{b}_{2}\end{array}\right),\quad p=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\underline{p}_{1}\\
\underline{p}_{2}\end{array}\right)

Similarly, for a
\mathbb{R}^{M\times n}
-matrix
\underline{X}
we call
X
its restriction to the upper left
2\times 2
square.
From (
8.37
)

\begin{split}\mathbb{R}^{2\times 2}\ni&\tilde{X}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}-\tilde{X}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1}\\
=&\sqrt{\delta}\left(\tilde{\kappa}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}+\tilde{\kappa}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1}-1\right)\big(\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}a^{\prime\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\otimes p^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{1-\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}}{1-\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}+\left(\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\right)^{2}}\langle\underline{\overline{b}},\frac{\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}}{|\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}|^{2}}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\overline{a}^{\prime}\otimes b^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\big)+o(\sqrt{\delta})\\
\end{split}

By
Lemma

A.4

\det_{2\times 2}(a\otimes b+c\otimes d)=-\langle a^{\perp},c\rangle\langle b^{\perp},d\rangle
, so

\begin{split}&\det_{2\times 2}\left(\tilde{X}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}-\tilde{X}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1}\right)\\
=&-\delta\,\left(\tilde{\kappa}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}+\tilde{\kappa}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}+1}-1\right)^{2}\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\frac{1}{2}\frac{1-\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}}{1-\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}+\left(\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\right)^{2}}\langle\underline{\overline{b}},\frac{\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}}{|\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}|^{2}}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\ \langle\left(a^{\prime\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\right)^{\perp},\overline{a}^{\prime}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\,\langle\left(p^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\right)^{\perp},b^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\\
&+o(\delta)\end{split}

And similarly,

\begin{split}&\det_{2\times 2}\left(\tilde{X}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+1}-\tilde{X}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+2}\right)\\
=&+\delta\,\left(\tilde{\kappa}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+1}+\tilde{\kappa}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}+2}-1\right)^{2}\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\frac{1}{2}\frac{1-\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}}{1-\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}+\left(\lambda^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\right)^{2}}\langle\underline{\overline{b}},\frac{\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}}{|\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}|^{2}}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\ \langle\left(a^{\prime\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\right)^{\perp},\overline{a}^{\prime}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\,\langle\left(p^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\right)^{\perp},b^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\\
&+o(\delta)\end{split}

So, in order to establish (
8.35
) we need

\langle\underline{\overline{b}},\frac{\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}}{|\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}|^{2}}\rangle\left(\sum_{\alpha,\beta=1}^{n}\left(\langle\underline{a}^{\prime\prime\mathfrak{i}_{1}}_{\alpha\beta},\underline{\overline{a}}^{\prime}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{M}}-\langle\underline{\overline{a}}_{\alpha\beta}^{\prime\prime},\underline{a}^{\prime\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{M}}\right)\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}_{\beta}\underline{b}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}_{\alpha}\right)>-2d^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\langle\underline{\overline{b}},\frac{\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}}{|\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}|^{2}}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\ \langle\left(a^{\prime\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\right)^{\perp},\overline{a}^{\prime}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\,\langle\left(p^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\right)^{\perp},b^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}

and in order to have (
8.36
) we need to assume

-\langle\underline{\overline{b}},\frac{\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}}{|\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}|^{2}}\rangle\left(\sum_{\alpha,\beta=1}^{n}\left(\langle\underline{a}^{\prime\prime\mathfrak{i}_{2}}_{\alpha\beta},\underline{\overline{a}}^{\prime}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{M}}-\langle\underline{\overline{a}}_{\alpha\beta}^{\prime\prime},\underline{a}^{\prime\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{M}}\right)\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}_{\beta}\underline{b}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}_{\alpha}\right)>+2d^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\langle\underline{\overline{b}},\frac{\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}}{|\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}|^{2}}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\ \langle\left(a^{\prime\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\right)^{\perp},\overline{a}^{\prime}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\,\langle\left(p^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\right)^{\perp},b^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}

This is exactly (
8.28
) and (
8.29
), so we can conclude.
∎

The conditions (
8.28
), (
8.29
) in
Proposition

8.9
are not too difficult to satisfy. We observe the following

Lemma 8.10
.

Assume we have a
T_{\aleph}
-configuration in
\mathbb{R}^{4\times 2}
that such that for two
C^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}=a^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\otimes b^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}
and
C^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}=a^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\otimes b^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}
(as usual
a^{\mathfrak{k}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}a^{\prime\mathfrak{k}}\\
a^{\prime\prime\mathfrak{k}}\end{array}\right)\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
,
b^{\mathfrak{k}}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
,
|b^{\mathfrak{k}}|=1
) we have

•

b^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}},b^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}
are linear independent in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
but not orthogonal to each other.

•

a^{\prime\mathfrak{i}_{1}},a^{\prime\mathfrak{i}_{2}}
are linear independent in
\mathbb{R}^{2}

then whatever
d^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}
,
d^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\in\mathbb{R}
is, we can find
\overline{a}\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
,
\overline{b}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
,
|\overline{b}|=1
such that (
8.28
), (
8.29
) are satisfied for the lifted version of the
T_{\aleph}
of
Lemma

8.6
.

Proof.

Set

\overline{b}\coloneqq\frac{\left(b^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\right)^{\perp}+\left(b^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\right)^{\perp}}{\left|b^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}+b^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\right|}.

Set

p^{\mathfrak{i}}\coloneqq\overline{b}-\langle\overline{b},b^{\mathfrak{i}}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}b^{\mathfrak{i}}.

Since
b^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}
and
b^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}
are linearly independent but not perpendicular in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
,

(8.38)

\begin{split}\langle\left(\overline{b}^{\mathfrak{i}}\right)^{\perp},p^{\mathfrak{i}}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}=&\langle\left(\overline{b}^{\mathfrak{i}}\right)^{\perp},\overline{b}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\\
=&\frac{1+\langle\left(b^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\right)^{\perp},\left(b^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\right)^{\perp}\rangle}{\left|b^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}+b^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\right|}\\
=&\frac{1}{2}\frac{\left|b^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}-\left(b^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\right)\right|^{2}}{\left|b^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}+b^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\right|}\\
=&\frac{\left|b^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}-b^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\right|}{2}\in(0,1)\\
\end{split}

\langle\overline{b},b^{\mathfrak{i}}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}=\pm\frac{\langle b^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}},\left(b^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\right)^{\perp}\rangle}{\left|b^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}+b^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\right|}\in(-1,1).

Indeed, the latter number is necessarily between
(-1,1)
: For this assume
b^{i_{1}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}1\\
0\end{array}\right)
then the above is (in absolute value)

\begin{split}&\frac{\sqrt{1-\theta^{2}}}{\sqrt{2+\theta}}<1\\
\Leftrightarrow\quad&1-\theta^{2}<2+\theta\\
\Leftrightarrow\quad&0<\theta^{2}+1+\theta\\
\end{split}

which is always true for
\theta\in[0,1]
.

Thus,

\langle\overline{b},p^{\mathfrak{i}}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}=1-\langle\overline{b},b^{\mathfrak{i}}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}^{2}\in(0,1)\quad\mathfrak{i}=\mathfrak{i}_{1},\mathfrak{i}_{2}.

Considering now the extension by zeroes
\underline{\overline{b}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
\underline{b}^{\mathfrak{i}}
we have for

\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}}\coloneqq\underline{\overline{b}}-\langle\underline{\overline{b}},\underline{b}^{\mathfrak{i}}\rangle\underline{b}^{\mathfrak{i}},

that

\langle\underline{\overline{b}},\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}=\langle\overline{b},p^{\mathfrak{i}}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}>0\quad\mathfrak{i}=\mathfrak{i}_{1},\mathfrak{i}_{2}.

Thus, in order to show (
8.28
) it suffices to show

\left(\sum_{\alpha,\beta=1}^{n}\left(\langle\underline{a}^{\prime\prime\mathfrak{i}_{1}}_{\alpha\beta},\underline{\overline{a}}^{\prime}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{M}}-\langle\underline{\overline{a}}_{\alpha\beta}^{\prime\prime},\underline{a}^{\prime\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{M}}\right)\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}_{\beta}\underline{b}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}_{\alpha}\right)>-2d^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\langle\left(a^{\prime\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\right)^{\perp},\overline{a}^{\prime}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\,\langle\left(p^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\right)^{\perp},b^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}

and for (
8.29
) it suffices to show

\left(\sum_{\alpha,\beta=1}^{n}\left(\langle\underline{a}^{\prime\prime\mathfrak{i}_{1}}_{\alpha\beta},\underline{\overline{a}}^{\prime}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{M}}-\langle\underline{\overline{a}}_{\alpha\beta}^{\prime\prime},\underline{a}^{\prime\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{M}}\right)\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}_{\beta}\underline{b}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}_{\alpha}\right)<-2d^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\langle\left(a^{\prime\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\right)^{\perp},\overline{a}^{\prime}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\,\langle\left(p^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\right)^{\perp},b^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}

If we choose
\overline{a}^{\prime}\asymp 0
, it actually suffices to establish

\langle\underline{\overline{a}}_{\alpha\beta}^{\prime\prime},\underline{a}^{\prime\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{M}}\,\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}_{\beta}\underline{b}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}_{\alpha}<0

and

\langle\underline{\overline{a}}_{\alpha\beta}^{\prime\prime},\underline{a}^{\prime\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{M}}\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}_{\beta}\underline{b}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}_{\alpha}>0

Take

\overline{a}^{\prime\prime}\coloneqq-\frac{a^{\prime\mathfrak{i}_{1}}}{\left|a^{\prime\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\right|}+\frac{a^{\prime\mathfrak{i}_{2}}}{\left|a^{\prime\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\right|}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}

then

\mathbb{R}^{M}\ni\underline{\overline{a}^{\prime\prime}}_{\alpha\beta}=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\overline{a}^{\prime\prime}\\
0_{M-2}\end{array}\right)\left(\delta_{\alpha 1}\delta_{\beta 2}-\delta_{\alpha 2}\delta_{\beta 1}\right)

Thus,

\langle\underline{\overline{a}}_{\alpha\beta}^{\prime\prime},\underline{a}^{\prime\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{M}}\,\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}_{\beta}\underline{b}^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}_{\alpha}=\langle\overline{a}^{\prime\prime},a^{\prime\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\underbrace{\langle\left(b^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\right)^{\perp},p^{\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}}_{>0}.

and

\langle\underline{\overline{a}}_{\alpha\beta}^{\prime\prime},\underline{a}^{\prime\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{M}}\underline{p}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}_{\beta}\underline{b}^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}_{\alpha}=\langle\overline{a}^{\prime\prime},a^{\prime\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\underbrace{\langle\left(b^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\right)^{\perp},p^{\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}}_{>0}.

Thus, it suffices to establish

\langle\overline{a}^{\prime\prime},a^{\prime\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}<0

and

\langle\overline{a}^{\prime\prime},a^{\prime\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}>0

Since
a^{\prime\mathfrak{i}_{1}}
and
a^{\prime\mathfrak{i}_{2}}
are linearly independent in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
we observe

\left|\left\langle\frac{a^{\prime\mathfrak{i}_{1}}}{\left|a^{\prime\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\right|},\frac{a^{\prime\mathfrak{i}_{2}}}{\left|a^{\prime\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\right|}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\right|<1

So we take

\overline{a}^{\prime\prime}\coloneqq-\frac{a^{\prime\mathfrak{i}_{1}}}{\left|a^{\prime\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\right|}+\frac{a^{\prime\mathfrak{i}_{2}}}{\left|a^{\prime\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\right|}

Then

\langle\overline{a}^{\prime\prime},\frac{a^{\prime\mathfrak{i}_{1}}}{\left|a^{\prime\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\right|}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}=-1+\left\langle\frac{a^{\prime\mathfrak{i}_{1}}}{\left|a^{\prime\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\right|},\frac{a^{\prime\mathfrak{i}_{2}}}{\left|a^{\prime\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\right|}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}<0

and

\langle\overline{a}^{\prime\prime},\frac{a^{\prime\mathfrak{i}_{2}}}{\left|a^{\prime\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\right|}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}=1-\left\langle\frac{a^{\prime\mathfrak{i}_{1}}}{\left|a^{\prime\mathfrak{i}_{1}}\right|},\frac{a^{\prime\mathfrak{i}_{2}}}{\left|a^{\prime\mathfrak{i}_{2}}\right|}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}>0

Thus (
8.28
), (
8.29
) are established. We can conclude.
∎

Proof of
Proposition

8.5
.

In
[

]
an example of a

-configuration in
\mathbb{R}^{4\times 2}
is given which satisfies (

, and the components

are given by

\begin{split}C_{1}=&\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}1&1\\

\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}1\\

\end{array}\right)\otimes\left(\begin{array}[]{c}1\\

\end{array}\right)\\

\end{array}\right)\\

\end{array}\right)\\

\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}-3\\

\end{array}\right)\otimes\left(\begin{array}[]{c}1\\

\end{array}\right)\\

\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}0\\

\end{array}\right)\\
\end{split}

So take e.g.
\mathfrak{i}_{1}=1
,

. Then

is linear independent to

, and and

satisfy (

), (

), and (

until we have a non-degenerate

) . We can conclude.

∎

9.
Proof of Theorem
1.1
and Theorem
1.2

Theorem

8.1
ensures the existence of some polyconvex
F_{0}
that has a non-degenerate
T_{\aleph}
-configuration.
Theorem

4.3
shows that we can distort
F_{0}
into some
F
such that
F
has all the previous properties, but additionally satisfies condition (C). By
Theorem

7.1
we find a non-
C^{1}
-solution to the Euler–Lagrange equation. Since
D^{2}F(\overline{X}_{\mathfrak{i}})
is positive definite and
{\rm dist\,}(du,\{\overline{Z}_{1},\ldots,\overline{Z}_{\aleph}))\ll 1
a.e. in
\Omega
we find that the assumptions of
[
10
, Theorem 6.1]
are satisfied, and thus
u
is a weak local minimizer. This proves
Theorem

1.1
.

As for
Theorem

1.2
: Taking
u_{2}\coloneqq{\rm Aff}
from
Theorem

7.1
we see that
{\rm Aff}
is a distributional solution of the Euler–Lagrange system (
2.1
) (since
D{\rm Aff}
is constant) and clearly
{\rm Aff}\not\equiv u
since
u
is nowhere
C^{1}
.

Appendix A
Basic facts from Analysis and Linear Algebra

A.1.
Analysis

Theorem A.1

(Vitali covering theorem)
.

Let
E\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
be a measurable set with finite Lebesgue measure. Assume that
\mathcal{V}
is a regular family of closed subsets
V\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
, which is a Vitali covering for
E
, i.e.

•

regular family:

{\rm diam\,}(V)^{d}\precsim\mathcal{L}(V)\quad\forall V\in\mathcal{V}.

•

Vitali covering:

\forall x\in E,\ \forall\delta>0\quad\exists V\in\mathcal{V}:\quad x\in V,\quad{\rm diam\,}(V)\in(0,\delta).

Then there exists an at most countable subcollection
(V_{j})_{j=1}^{\infty}\subset\mathcal{V}
which is pairwise disjoint and

\mathcal{L}^{d}(E\setminus\bigcup_{j}U_{j})=0.

Lemma A.2
.

Let
f:\mathcal{M}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{N}
be a smooth map which around a point
\overline{x}
is bi-Lipschitz, i.e.

\limsup_{y\to\overline{x}}\frac{|f(\overline{x})-f(y)|}{|\overline{x}-y|}\geq\lambda>0

Then there exists an
\varepsilon>0
such that
f\Big|_{B(x,\varepsilon)\cap\mathcal{M}^{d}}
is a smooth diffeomorphism onto its own image.

Proof.

For any
v\in T_{\overline{x}}\mathcal{M}^{d}
with
|v|=1
we have

\lambda\leq\lim_{t\to 0}\frac{|f(\overline{x}+tv)-f(\overline{x})|}{t}=|Df(\overline{x})v|.

In particular
Df(\overline{x})
is injective, i.e. has full rank. Thus, by the implicit function theorem, we have that
f
is locally a diffeomorphism onto its target.
∎

A.2.
Linear Algebra

Lemma A.3
.

Let
X,Y\subset Z
be two linearly independent spaces such that
Z=X+Y
.

Assume
A:X\to Z
and
B:Y\to Z

S\coloneqq\{z\in Z:z=x+y,\quad Ax=By\}

Then

\dim S\leq\dim\ker A+\dim Y.

Proof.

We observe that

\tilde{A}\coloneqq A\Big|_{(\ker A)^{\perp}}\to Z

is injective and thus for
T\coloneqq{\rm im\,}\tilde{A}
we have

\tilde{A}:\ker A^{\perp}\to T

is bijective.

Take a basis
(\overline{x}_{1},\ldots,\overline{x}_{K})
of
\ker A
and

Assume
z=x+y\in S
for some
x\in X
and
y\in Y
. Then (by uniqueness of decomposition)
Ax=By
.
Set

\tilde{x}\coloneqq x-\overline{x}_{i}\langle\overline{x}_{i},x\rangle\in(\ker A)^{\perp}

We still have

\tilde{A}\tilde{x}=A\tilde{x}=By.

In particular,
By\in T
, and thus we have

\tilde{x}=\tilde{A}^{-1}By.

We have shown

z=(x-\tilde{x})+(I+\tilde{A}^{-1}B)y\subset

That is, for
C\coloneqq(I+\tilde{A}^{-1}B)
we have shown

z\in\ker_{X}A+{\rm im\,}_{Y}C.

Since
C
is a linear operation on
Y
, we have that
\dim{\rm im\,}_{Y}C\leq\dim Y
. We can conclude.

∎

Lemma A.4
.

Let
a,b,c,d\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
. Recall the notation
v^{\perp}=(-v_{2},v_{1})^{T}
. Then

\det(a\otimes b-c\otimes d)=\langle a^{\perp},c\rangle\langle b^{\perp},d\rangle

Proof.

a\otimes b-c\otimes d=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}a^{1}b^{1}&a^{1}b^{2}\\
a^{2}b^{1}&a^{2}b^{2}\end{array}\right)+\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}c^{1}d^{1}&c^{1}d^{2}\\
c^{2}d^{1}&c^{2}d^{2}\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}a^{1}b^{1}+c^{1}d^{1}&a^{1}b^{2}+c^{1}d^{2}\\
a^{2}b^{1}+c^{2}d^{1}&a^{2}b^{2}+c^{2}d^{2}\end{array}\right)

So

\begin{split}\det(a\otimes b-c\otimes d)=&\left(a^{1}b^{1}+c^{1}d^{1}\right)\left(a^{2}b^{2}+c^{2}d^{2}\right)-\left(a^{1}b^{2}+c^{1}d^{2}\right)\left(a^{2}b^{1}+c^{2}d^{1}\right)\\
=&a^{1}c^{2}\left(b^{1}d^{2}-b^{2}d^{1}\right)\\
&+a^{2}c^{1}\left(d^{1}b^{2}-d^{2}b^{1}\right)\\
=&a^{1}c^{2}\langle b^{\perp},d\rangle-a^{2}c^{1}\langle b^{\perp},d\rangle\\
=&\langle a^{\perp},c\rangle\langle b^{\perp},d\rangle\\
\end{split}

∎

Lemma A.5
.

Let

J=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&-1\\
1&0\end{array}\right)

Recall the notation
v^{\perp}=(-v_{2},v_{1})^{T}
.
Then

\left(a\otimes b\right)J=-a\otimes b^{\perp},

and thus

\langle\left(a\otimes b\right)J,c\otimes d\rangle=-\langle a,c\rangle\,\langle b^{\perp},d\rangle

Proof.

We have

a\otimes b=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}a^{1}b^{1}&a^{1}b^{2}\\
a^{2}b^{1}&a^{2}b^{2}\\
\end{array}\right)

and

a\otimes b^{\perp}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}-a^{1}b^{2}&a^{1}b^{1}\\
-a^{2}b^{2}&a^{2}b^{1}\\
\end{array}\right)

So

a\otimes bJ=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}a^{1}b^{1}&a^{1}b^{2}\\
a^{2}b^{1}&a^{2}b^{2}\\
\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&-1\\
1&0\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}a^{1}b^{2}&-a^{1}b^{1}\\
a^{2}b^{2}&-a^{2}b^{1}\end{array}\right)

∎

Appendix B
Polyconvexity, rank one convexity, Legendre-Hadamard condition

Definition B.1
.

A map
F\in C^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{M\times n},\mathbb{R})
satisfies the Legendre-Hadamard condition if

\partial_{X_{i\alpha}}\partial_{X_{j\beta}}F(X)\xi_{i}\xi_{j}\,\eta_{\alpha}\eta_{\beta}\geq 0

for all
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{M}
and
\eta\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

F
satisfies the strong Legendre-Hadamard condition if for some
\lambda>0

(B.1)

\partial_{X_{i\alpha}}\partial_{X_{j\beta}}F(X)\xi_{i}\xi_{j}\,\eta_{\alpha}\eta_{\beta}\geq\lambda|\xi|_{\mathbb{R}^{M}}^{2}|\eta|_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}^{2}

for all
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{M}
and
\eta\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

It is important not to confuse the Legendre-Hadamard-condition with convexity which takes the form

\partial_{X_{i\alpha}}\partial_{X_{j\beta}}F(X)X_{i\alpha}X_{j\beta}\geq 0\quad\forall X\in\mathbb{R}^{M\times n}

Lemma B.2
.

Assume that
F
satisfies the strong Legendre-Hadamard condition and fix
X\in\mathbb{R}^{M\times n}
.

\mathbb{R}^{M\times n}\ni Z\mapsto D^{2}F(X)Z\in\mathbb{R}^{M\times n}

is a bijective linear map.

Proof.

D^{2}F(X)
is a linear map from
\mathbb{R}^{M\times n}\to\mathbb{R}^{M\times n}
defined via

\mathbb{R}^{M\times n}\ni Z\mapsto D^{2}F(X)Z=\left(\sum_{j=1}^{M}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}\partial_{X_{i\alpha}}\partial_{X_{j\beta}}F(X)Z_{j\beta}\right)_{i\in\{1,\ldots,M\},\alpha\in\{1,\ldots,n\}}.

It has full rank if it is injective, i.e. if
\ker D^{2}F=\{0\}
.

Let
\xi^{1},\ldots,\xi^{M}
be an orthonormal basis of
\mathbb{R}^{M}
and
\eta^{1},\ldots,\eta^{n}
an orthonormal basis of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
.
Then

\left(\xi^{i}\otimes\eta^{\alpha}\right)_{i=1,\ldots,M;\alpha=1,\ldots,n}

is an orthonormal basis of
\mathbb{R}^{M\times n}
– orthonormal in the sense of the Hilbert-Schmidt scalar product.

By the strong Legendre-Hadamard condition we have

\langle D^{2}F(X)\xi^{i}\otimes\eta^{\alpha},\xi^{i}\otimes\eta^{\alpha}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{M\times n}}>0

and thus the image
D^{2}F(X)
is
\mathbb{R}^{M\times n}
. Thus
Z\mapsto D^{2}F(X)Z
is surjective, thus as a linear map between the same space
Z\mapsto D^{2}F(X)Z
must be bijective.
∎

Lemma B.3
.

Assume
F\in C^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{M\times n},\mathbb{R})
is rank-one convex, i.e. assume that for any
X,Y\in\mathbb{R}^{M\times n}
with
{\rm rank\,}(X-Y)\leq 1
we have

F(\lambda X+(1-\lambda)Y)\leq\lambda F(X)+(1-\lambda)F(Y)\quad\forall\lambda\in[0,1].

Then
F
satisfies the Legendre-Hadamard condition.

Proof.

Fix
X\in\mathbb{R}^{M\times n}
,
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
and
\eta\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
and set

\phi(t)\coloneqq F(X+t\xi\otimes\eta).

Then

\phi(\lambda t_{0}+(1-\lambda)t_{1})=F(\lambda(X+t_{0}\xi\otimes\eta)+(1-\lambda)(X+t_{1}\xi\otimes\eta)),

and thus
\phi
is convex. This implies

0\leq\phi^{\prime\prime}(t)=\partial_{X_{i\alpha}}\partial_{X_{j\beta}}F(X)\xi_{i}\xi_{j}\,\eta_{\alpha}\eta_{\beta}.

∎

Recall that a function
F:\mathbb{R}^{M\times n}\to\mathbb{R}
is called polyconvex if the map

\mathbb{R}^{M\times n}\ni X\mapsto F(X)

can be written as a convex function of the
\ell\times\ell
subdeterminants of
X
,
\ell\in\{1,\ldots,\min\{n,M\}\}
. And
F
is strongly polyconvex if
F(X)-\gamma|X|^{2}
is polyconvex for some
\gamma>0
.

Lemma B.4
.

Assume that
F\in C^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{M\times n})
is polyconvex. Then
F
is rank-one convex, and thus
F
satisfies the Legendre-Hadamard condition.

If
F
is strongly polyconvex, then
F
satisfies the strong Legendre-Hadamard condition.

Proof.

Fix
t\in(0,1)
,
X,Y\in\mathbb{R}^{M\times n}
with
{\rm rank\,}(X-Y)\leq 1
. Fix any
k\times k
subspace of
\mathbb{R}^{M\times n}
, and denote by
X^{\prime}
,
Y^{\prime}
the projections into this subspace identified with
\mathbb{R}^{k\times k}
.

Set

\varphi(t)\coloneqq\det_{k\times k}(tX^{\prime}+(1-t)Y^{\prime})

Since
{\rm rank\,}(X^{\prime}-Y^{\prime})\leq 1
the map
\varphi
is affine linear in
t
. Moreover we know
\varphi(1)=\det_{k\times k}(X^{\prime})
and
\varphi(0)=\det(Y^{\prime})
. We conclude

\det_{k\times k}(tX^{\prime}+(1-t)Y^{\prime})=t\det_{k\times k}X^{\prime}+(1-t)\det_{k\times k}Y^{\prime}.\quad t\in\mathbb{R}.

Since
F
is polyconvex it can be written as as a convex function
k\times k
subdeterminants of
X

F(X)=G(X,\det_{1\times 1}(X^{\prime}),\ldots,\det_{k\times k}(X^{\prime}),\ldots).

Since by the above argument all the (sub-)determinants behave linearly on
tX+(1-t)Y
,
F
is rank-one convex by convexity of
G
.

Thus,
F
satisfies the Legendre-Hadamard condition by
Lemma

B.3
.

If
F
is moreover strongly polyconvex, then
F(X)=\varepsilon|X|^{2}+\tilde{F}
, where
\tilde{F}
is polyconvex. Since
\tilde{F}
thus satisfies the Legendre-Hadamard condition we find

\partial_{X_{i\alpha}}\partial_{X_{j\beta}}F(X)\xi_{i}\xi_{j}\,\eta_{\alpha}\eta_{\beta}\geq 2\varepsilon\,|\xi|^{2}_{\mathbb{R}^{M}}|\eta|^{2}_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}.

Thus a strongly polyconvex
F
satisfies the strong Legendre-Hadamard condition.
∎

As a corollary of the previous lemmata we have

Lemma B.5
.

Assume that
F\in C^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{M\times n})
is strongly polyconvex. Then

\mathbb{R}^{M\times n}\ni Z\mapsto D^{2}F(X)Z\in\mathbb{R}^{M\times n}

is a bijective linear map.

Appendix C
Explicit formulas for
T_{\aleph}
-configurations

Following the ideas of
[
14
]
, using Mathematica we computed explicit formulas for
T_{\aleph}
-configurations that satisfy the conclusions of
Proposition

8.5
.
